PART I: FOUNDATION
Chapter 1: Purpose & Principles
1.1 Core Purpose

Enable anyone, anywhere to generate usable electricity from commonly accessible materials through the simplest possible, most reliable, and scalable design.

This handbook provides complete instructions for building functional electricity generators using:

    Magnets (scavenged from speakers, hard drives, motors, toys)
    Copper wire (scavenged from cables, transformers, motors, appliances)
    Common materials (wood, plastic, metal, found objects)
    Basic hand tools (screwdriver, pliers, saw, drill)

1.2 Design Principles
Principle	Implementation
Simplicity	Minimum viable components (6 core parts)
Accessibility	Materials found globally in discarded items
Tolerance	Works despite imprecise construction
Scalability	Modular design: 1W → 1kW
Safety	Built-in protection, clear warnings
Sustainability	100% recyclable, renewable operation
Openness	Free, patent-free, share freely
1.3 What You Can Power
Generator Output	Examples
1-5W	LED lights, small radio, phone charging (slow)
5-20W	Phone charging (normal), LED strips, small fans
20-50W	Laptop charging, router, LED TV
50-100W	Power tools (light), refrigerator (small/efficient)
100-500W	Multiple devices, small household
500W+	Significant household load (advanced design)
1.4 The Physics (Simplified)

Faraday's Law: Moving a magnet past a coil of wire creates electricity.

text

VOLTAGE = (Number of Wire Turns) × (Magnet Strength) × (Speed of Movement)
POWER = VOLTAGE × CURRENT

To increase output:

    More wire turns (more voltage)
    Stronger magnets (more flux)
    Faster rotation (more change)
    Thicker wire (more current capacity)
    Smaller air gap (more flux reaches coil)

Chapter 2: Materials Sourcing Guide
2.1 Magnet Sources (Ranked by Strength)
Tier 1: EXCELLENT (Neodymium - NdFeB)
Source	Typical Quantity	Strength	Notes
Hard disk drives	2 arc magnets	Very strong	Best source, handle carefully
Computer fans (brushless)	4-8 small	Strong	Ring or individual
Cordless tool motors	4 arcs	Very strong	Large, powerful
Headphones (over-ear)	2 discs	Strong	Small but powerful
Magnetic phone mounts	1-4	Strong	Often ring shaped
Tier 2: GOOD (Ferrite - Ceramic)
Source	Typical Quantity	Strength	Notes
Large speakers	1-2 rings	Medium-strong	Large, heavy
Small speakers	2-4 discs	Medium	Common, useful
Old motors (AC)	Multiple arcs	Medium	Curved, harder to use
Magnetic door catches	2-4	Medium	Rectangular, easy to handle
Tier 3: USABLE (Various)
Source	Typical Quantity	Strength	Notes
Refrigerator magnets	Many	Weak	Need many, last resort
Magnetic toys	Variable	Weak-medium	Often small
Bag clasps	2-4	Weak	Very small

⚠️ SAFETY WARNING: Strong magnets can:

    Pinch fingers severely (blood blisters, crushing)
    Shatter if they collide (flying fragments)
    Erase data on credit cards, hard drives
    Interfere with pacemakers
    Be lethal if swallowed (especially multiple)

KEEP AWAY FROM CHILDREN
2.2 Wire Sources
Source	Wire Gauge (AWG)	Insulation	Length Available
Extension cords	14-18	Rubber/PVC (good)	5-50m
Lamp cords	18-20	PVC (good)	2-5m
Power cables (computer)	16-18	PVC (good)	1-2m
Transformers (unwound)	20-28	Enamel (excellent)	10-200m
Motor windings	18-24	Enamel (excellent)	10-100m
Telephone wire	22-26	PVC (good)	Variable
Ethernet cables	24	PVC (thin)	1-50m
Coaxial cable (center)	18-22	Copper (bare)	1-100m

Best Sources:

    Dead transformers (microwave, power supply) - most wire, best insulation
    Dead motors - good wire, already wound
    Extension cords - thick wire, easy to strip

Wire Gauge Guide:

    Thicker wire (lower AWG number) = More current capacity, less resistance
    Thinner wire (higher AWG number) = More turns per space, higher voltage

Source	Bearing Type	Quality	Notes
Skateboard/rollerblade	Ball bearing 608	Excellent	Standard size, widely available
Bicycle wheels	Ball bearing	Excellent	Large, may need adapting
Hard disk drives	Ball bearing	Excellent	Small but precise
Fidget spinners	Ball bearing 608	Good	Same as skateboard
CD/DVD drives	Ball/sleeve	Good	Small, delicate
Electric motors	Ball/sleeve	Good	Various sizes
Drawer slides	Ball bearing	Good	Linear but adaptable
Car parts (alternator, water pump)	Ball bearing	Excellent	Large, durable

If No Bearings Available:
See Section 4.6 for DIY bearing alternatives.

Material	Sources	Uses

Metal (steel sheet)	Appliances, cans, car parts	Magnetic circuit backing

PART II: CORE DESIGN
Chapter 4: The OPTIBEST Generator Architecture
4.1 Design Selection Rationale

After systematic evaluation of generator topologies:
Topology	Simplicity	Efficiency	Scalability	DIY Feasibility	Score
Axial Flux (SELECTED)	★★★★★	★★★★	★★★★★	★★★★★	24
Radial Flux	★★★	★★★★	★★★★	★★★	18
Linear	★★★★★	★★	★★	★★★★★	18
Transverse Flux	★★	★★★★★	★★★	★	15

Axial Flux Advantages:

    Flat geometry = easy to build
    Low RPM capable = hand/wind/water driveable
    Naturally modular = easy scaling
    Tolerance-friendly = works despite imprecision
    Simple bearing requirements = one central shaft

4.2 Core Architecture

text

                    AXIAL FLUX GENERATOR - SIDE VIEW
                    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │                   STATOR 2                   │
        │    ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐       │
        │    │COIL │  │COIL │  │COIL │  │COIL │       │
        │    └─────┘  └─────┘  └─────┘  └─────┘       │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                           ↑ AIR GAP (3-10mm)
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │    [N]      [S]      [N]      [S]    ROTOR  │
        │    MAG      MAG      MAG      MAG          │
        └──────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                           │ SHAFT
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │    ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐       │
        │    │COIL │  │COIL │  │COIL │  │COIL │       │
        │    └─────┘  └─────┘  └─────┘  └─────┘       │
        │                   STATOR 1                   │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

                    AXIAL FLUX GENERATOR - TOP VIEW

                     ╱               ╲

              MAGNETS: Alternating N-S-N-S-N-S around disc
              COILS: Positioned between magnet positions

4.3 The 6 Core Components
#	Component	Function	Material Options
1	Rotor Disc	Carries magnets, rotates	Wood, plastic, aluminum
2	Magnet Array	Creates magnetic field	Scavenged magnets
3	Stator Ring	Holds coils stationary	Wood, plastic
4	Coil Array	Converts flux to electricity	Copper/aluminum wire
5	Shaft Assembly	Rotation axis + bearings	Steel rod, pipe + bearings
6	Frame	Holds everything in alignment	Wood, metal, plastic
4.4 Scaling Configurations
TIER 0: Minimal (1-5W)

    4 magnets, 4 coils
    Hand crank drive
    Phone charging, LED lighting

TIER 1: Basic (5-20W)

    8 magnets, 8 coils
    Hand/pedal drive
    Multiple device charging

TIER 2: Standard (20-100W)

    12-16 magnets, 12-16 coils (or dual rotor)
    Wind/water/pedal drive
    Household LED lighting, small appliances

TIER 3: Advanced (100-500W)

    Multiple stacked units
    Dedicated drive (wind turbine, water wheel, engine)
    Significant household load

TIER 4: Full (500W+)

    Commercial-scale build
    Requires precision construction
    Beyond this handbook scope (reference only)

4.5 Dimension Calculator

Base Formula:

text

Rotor Diameter = 4 × (Magnet Width) + Shaft Diameter + Margins
Stator Diameter = Rotor Diameter + 2 × (Coil Width)

Example Calculations:
Magnet Size	Shaft	Rotor Ø	Stator Ø	Target Output
20mm	12mm	100mm	160mm	5-10W
30mm	16mm	150mm	230mm	15-30W
40mm	20mm	200mm	300mm	30-60W
50mm	25mm	250mm	370mm	50-100W

Recommended Proportions:

    Air gap: 3-10mm (smaller = more power, harder to maintain)
    Magnet thickness: 5-20mm (thicker = stronger field)
    Coil height: 10-40mm (match magnet thickness approximately)
    Shaft diameter: 8-25mm (match available bearings)

4.6 Bearing Options (Tiered by Availability)
Option A: Ball Bearings (BEST)

Source: Skateboard, bicycle, hard drives, motors, fidget spinners

text

                    BALL BEARING MOUNTING
                    
        ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
        │              FRAME                     │
        │         ┌───────────┐                 │
        │         │  BEARING  │                 │
        │         │    ┌─┐    │                 │
        │         │    │█│ ← SHAFT              │
        │         │    └─┘    │                 │
        │         └───────────┘                 │
        └───────────────────────────────────────┘

Installation:

    Measure bearing outer diameter
    Drill or cut hole to match (tight fit)
    Press or tap bearing into hole
    Bearing inner race fits on shaft

Option B: Bottle Cap Bearing (ACCESSIBLE)

Source: Plastic bottle caps, any smooth plastic

text

                    BOTTLE CAP BEARING
                    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │              FRAME                       │
        │         ╭───────────╮                   │
        │         │  ┌─────┐  │ ← Bottle cap      │
        │         │  │ WAX │  │   (upside down)   │
        │         │  │  █  │  │                   │
        │         │  │SHAFT│  │                   │
        │         │  └─────┘  │                   │
        │         ╰───────────╯                   │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────┘

Construction:

    Cut hole in bottle cap center (matches shaft diameter)
    Fill cap with wax, soap, or grease
    Insert shaft through cap
    Mount cap in frame with opening facing shaft
    Lubricate regularly

Option C: Wood Journal Bearing (PRIMITIVE)

Source: Hardwood blocks

text

                    WOOD JOURNAL BEARING
                    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │              FRAME                       │
        │         ┌───────────┐                   │
        │         │  HARDWOOD │                   │
        │         │   ╭───╮   │                   │
        │         │   │ █ │ ← Drilled hole       │
        │         │   ╰───╯   │   (waxed)         │
        │         └───────────┘                   │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────┘

Construction:

    Select hardwood (oak, maple, or similar)
    Drill hole 1-2mm larger than shaft
    Soak in oil or apply wax/soap generously
    Re-lubricate every 10-50 hours of use

Option D: Magnetic Bearing (ADVANCED)

Source: Requires extra magnets

text

                    MAGNETIC BEARING
                    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │              FRAME                       │
        │         ┌───[N]───┐                     │
        │         │         │ ← Fixed magnet     │
        │         │    ↕    │   (repels shaft    │
        │         │   [N]   │    magnet)         │
        │         │    █    │                     │
        │         │  SHAFT  │                     │
        │         └─────────┘                     │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────┘
        
        Note: Requires lateral constraint (guide rails)

Construction:

    Mount ring magnet on shaft
    Mount opposing ring magnet in frame (same polarity facing)
    Magnetic repulsion provides axial support
    Add simple guide (pins, rails) for lateral constraint
    Zero friction, infinite life

Chapter 5: Detailed Construction
5.1 COMPONENT 1: Rotor Disc

Function: Carries magnets, attaches to shaft, rotates

Material Options:
Material	Pros	Cons	Suitability
Plywood (12-18mm)	Easy to cut, available	Can warp if wet	★★★★★
MDF	Flat, smooth	Heavy, weak when wet	★★★★
Plastic (HDPE, PVC)	Waterproof, durable	Harder to cut	★★★★★
Aluminum	Strong, light	Eddy current losses	★★★
Steel	Magnetic backing	Heavy, eddy currents	★★

RECOMMENDED: Plywood or HDPE plastic

Construction Steps:

text

STEP 1: Mark Center and Outer Circle

        ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
        │                                 │
        │           ╭───────╮             │
        │         ╱    ●     ╲            │ ← Use compass, string,
        │        │   CENTER    │          │    or nail + string
        │         ╲           ╱           │
        │           ╰───────╯             │
        │                                 │
        └─────────────────────────────────┘

text

STEP 2: Cut Outer Circle

        Hand saw: Cut straight lines to rough octagon, then round
        Jigsaw: Follow line directly
        Knife: Score repeatedly (plastic only)
        
        ╭───────────────────╮
        │                   │
        │                   │   Sand or file edges smooth
        │        ●          │
        │                   │
        │                   │
        ╰───────────────────╯

text

STEP 3: Drill Center Hole

        Hole diameter = Shaft diameter + 0.5mm (tight fit)
        
        If no drill: Heat nail/rod, push through
        If no exact size: Drill small, enlarge with file/knife
        
        ╭───────────────────╮
        │                   │
        │       ╭─╮         │
        │       │ │         │   Hole should be centered
        │       ╰─╯         │   and perpendicular
        │                   │
        ╰───────────────────╯

text

STEP 4: Mark Magnet Positions

        Divide circle evenly based on magnet count:
        
        4 magnets = 90° apart
        6 magnets = 60° apart  
        8 magnets = 45° apart
        12 magnets = 30° apart
        
        ╭───────────────────╮
        │     [1]           │
        │                   │
        │ [4]    ●    [2]   │   Mark centers at consistent
        │                   │   radius from center
        │     [3]           │
        ╰───────────────────╯

Magnet Position Formula:

text

Radius for magnets = (Disc radius - Magnet width) / 2 + Shaft hole radius / 2
Angle between magnets = 360° / Number of magnets

5.2 COMPONENT 2: Magnet Array

Function: Creates alternating magnetic poles that sweep past coils

CRITICAL: Magnets must be arranged in alternating polarity:

text

CORRECT (Alternating N-S-N-S):

        ╭───────────────────────╮
        │     [N]               │
        │                       │
        │ [S]     ●     [S]     │   Each magnet's north pole
        │                       │   faces opposite direction
        │     [N]               │   from its neighbors
        ╰───────────────────────╯

INCORRECT (All same direction):

        ╭───────────────────────╮
        │     [N]               │
        │                       │
        │ [N]     ●     [N]     │   ← WILL NOT WORK
        │                       │   (No flux change)
        │     [N]               │
        ╰───────────────────────╯

Testing Polarity:

    Mark one side of first magnet as "UP"
    Bring second magnet close:
        ATTRACTS = opposite poles, mount opposite direction
        REPELS = same poles, mount same direction
    Alternate mounting direction around disc

Mounting Methods:
Method A: Epoxy (BEST)

text

1. Clean magnet and disc surface
2. Roughen disc surface (sandpaper)
3. Mix 2-part epoxy
4. Apply thin layer to disc
5. Press magnet firmly, hold 30 seconds
6. Allow 24 hours cure before use

        ┌─────────────────────┐
        │█████████████████████│ ← Magnet
        │▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒▒│ ← Epoxy layer
        │░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░│ ← Rotor disc
        └─────────────────────┘

Method B: Mechanical Retention (RELIABLE)

text

1. Create pocket slightly smaller than magnet
2. Press-fit magnet into pocket
3. Add cover plate over magnets

        ┌─────────────────────┐
        │░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░│ ← Cover plate
        ├────┬──────────┬─────┤
        │    │██████████│     │ ← Magnet in pocket
        │    │██████████│     │
        └────┴──────────┴─────┘
             ← Pocket walls →

Method C: Tape + Screws (ACCESSIBLE)

text

1. Wrap magnet in tape (prevents shattering)
2. Place on disc in marked position
3. Tape magnet to disc
4. Add screw/nail at edge as stop

        ┌───────────────┐
        │   TAPE        │
        │ ┌───────────┐ │
        │ │  MAGNET   │●│ ← Screw prevents sliding
        │ └───────────┘ │
        │      on DISC  │
        └───────────────┘

SAFETY NOTES:

    Neodymium magnets shatter if they collide - handle carefully
    Never let magnets snap together uncontrolled
    Wear eye protection during assembly
    Keep away from electronics (hard drives, credit cards)

5.3 COMPONENT 3: Stator Ring

Function: Holds coils in fixed position, provides structural support

Material Requirements:

    Non-magnetic (no steel)
    Rigid
    Insulating (preferred)

Recommended Materials:
Material	Suitability	Notes
Plywood	★★★★★	Easy to work, insulating
MDF	★★★★	Flat, easy to work
Plastic (any)	★★★★★	Waterproof, insulating
Aluminum	★★★	Conductive but non-magnetic

text

STATOR RING DIMENSIONS:

        ╭─────────────────────────────╮
        │                             │
        │    ╭─────────────────╮      │
        │    │                 │      │
        │    │    (Center      │      │
        │    │     Clear)      │      │
        │    │                 │      │
        │    ╰─────────────────╯      │
        │                             │
        ╰─────────────────────────────╯
        
        Outer Diameter = Rotor Diameter + 2 × Coil Width + 20mm margin
        Inner Diameter = Rotor Diameter - 2 × Coil Width - 20mm margin
        Ring Width = 2 × Coil Width + 40mm

Construction Steps:

text

STEP 1: Cut Outer Circle
        (Same as rotor, larger diameter)

STEP 2: Cut Inner Circle
        Option A: Jigsaw from inside
        Option B: Drill many holes around inner circle, break out center, file smooth
        
        ╭───────────────────────────╮
        │   ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○       │
        │ ○                   ○     │
        │ ○                   ○     │
        │ ○                   ○     │ ← Drill holes around inner
        │ ○                   ○     │    then knock out center
        │   ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○ ○       │
        ╰───────────────────────────╯

text

STEP 3: Mark Coil Positions

        Coils sit between magnet positions (offset by half spacing)
        
        If magnets at 0°, 90°, 180°, 270°
        Then coils at 45°, 135°, 225°, 315°
        
              [M]                    [C] = Coil position
           [C]   [C]                 [M] = Magnet position
        [M]    ●    [M]
           [C]   [C]
              [M]

5.4 COMPONENT 4: Coil Array

Function: Converts changing magnetic flux into electrical current

Wire Gauge Selection:
Available Wire	Voltage per Coil	Current Capacity	Best For
AWG 18 (1mm)	Lower	Higher (8A)	High current, low RPM
AWG 20 (0.8mm)	Medium	Medium (5A)	Balanced
AWG 22 (0.6mm)	Higher	Lower (3A)	Higher voltage
AWG 24 (0.5mm)	Highest	Lowest (2A)	Maximum voltage

Coil Design Formula:

text

Turns per coil = (Available wire length) / (4 × Coil width)
Voltage per coil ≈ Turns × Flux × RPM / 60,000,000 × constant

Simplified Guide:
Target Output	Wire Gauge	Turns per Coil	Coils Needed
5V (USB)	AWG 20-22	50-100	4-8
12V	AWG 20-22	100-200	4-8
24V+	AWG 22-24	200-400	8+

Coil Winding Methods:
Method A: Hand Wound (Simple)

text

STEP 1: Make winding form
        Cut cardboard/plastic rectangle
        
        Width = Coil inner width
        Height = Coil height × 2 (folded)
        
        ┌─────────────────────┐
        │                     │
        │                     │
        │                     │
        └─────────────────────┘
        
        Fold in half, tape edges
        
        ┌───────────┐
        │           │
        │           │
        └───────────┘

STEP 2: Wind wire around form
        
        Leave 30cm lead at start
        Wind tightly, counting turns
        Keep turns neat (side by side, layers on top)
        Leave 30cm lead at end
        
          lead →  ┌────────────┐
                  │ ╔════════╗ │
                  │ ║████████║ │
                  │ ║████████║ │ ← Wire wound around form
                  │ ║████████║ │
                  │ ╚════════╝ │
           lead → └────────────┘

STEP 3: Secure coil
        Wrap finished coil with tape
        Slide off form
        Form is reusable for next coil
        
          ┌────────────┐
          │ ████████ │
          │ ████████ │  ← Tape wrapped coil
          │ ████████ │
          └────────────┘

Method B: Direct Wound (No Form)

text

Wind directly onto stator:

1. Drill two holes for wire feed
2. Thread wire through
3. Wind around marked area
4. Tie off and secure with tape/epoxy

        ┌───────────────────────────┐
        │         ╭───╮             │
        │  ○─────│|||│─────○       │ ← Wire through holes
        │        │|||│             │
        │        │|||│             │ ← Wound around stator section
        │        │|||│             │
        │         ╰───╯             │
        └───────────────────────────┘

Coil Connection Patterns:
Series Connection (Higher Voltage)

text

All coils in series: End of coil 1 → Start of coil 2, etc.

    ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐
  ──┤ C1  ├───┤ C2  ├───┤ C3  ├───┤ C4  ├──
    └─────┘   └─────┘   └─────┘   └─────┘
    
Output voltage = Sum of all coil voltages
Output current = Single coil current

Parallel Connection (Higher Current)

text

All coils in parallel: All starts together, all ends together

        ┌─────┐
      ──┤ C1  ├──┐
        └─────┘  │
        ┌─────┐  │
    ┌───┤ C2  ├──┼───┐
    │   └─────┘  │   │
    │   ┌─────┐  │   │
    ├───┤ C3  ├──┤   │
    │   └─────┘  │   │
    │   ┌─────┐  │   │
    └───┤ C4  ├──┘   │
        └─────┘      │
                     │
    
Output voltage = Single coil voltage
Output current = Sum of all coil currents

Series-Parallel (Balanced)

text

Groups in series, groups paralleled:

        ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐
    ┌───┤ C1  ├───┤ C2  ├───┐
    │   └─────┘   └─────┘   │
    │                       ├── Output
    │   ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐   │
    └───┤ C3  ├───┤ C4  ├───┘
        └─────┘   └─────┘
    
Output voltage = 2 × Single coil voltage
Output current = 2 × Single coil current

Three-Phase Configuration (RECOMMENDED for 6+ coils)

text

THREE-PHASE STAR CONNECTION:

              Phase A
                 │
            ┌────┴────┐
            │   C1    │
            │   C4    │ (in series)
            └────┬────┘
                 │
    Phase B ─────●───── Phase C
                 │
              NEUTRAL
              (optional)

    Coil Assignment (12 coils):
    Phase A: Coils 1, 4, 7, 10
    Phase B: Coils 2, 5, 8, 11  
    Phase C: Coils 3, 6, 9, 12
    
    Each phase offset by 120 electrical degrees

Why Three-Phase?

    Smoother power output (less pulsing)
    Higher efficiency rectification
    Better power density
    Industry standard (easier integration)

Coil Mounting

text

METHOD A: Surface Mount (Simple)

    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
    │          STATOR RING            │
    │                                 │
    │    ┌───┐  ┌───┐  ┌───┐  ┌───┐  │
    │    │ C │  │ C │  │ C │  │ C │  │ ← Coils glued/taped
    │    └───┘  └───┘  └───┘  └───┘  │   on surface
    │                                 │
    └─────────────────────────────────┘

METHOD B: Embedded (Better)

    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░ │
    │ ░░░┌───┐░░┌───┐░░┌───┐░░┌───┐░░ │
    │ ░░░│ C │░░│ C │░░│ C │░░│ C │░░ │ ← Coils in routed
    │ ░░░└───┘░░└───┘░░└───┘░░└───┘░░ │   pockets
    │ ░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░░ │
    └─────────────────────────────────┘

METHOD C: Potted (Best protection)

    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
    │ ▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓ │
    │ ▓▓▓┌───┐▓▓┌───┐▓▓┌───┐▓▓┌───┐▓▓ │
    │ ▓▓▓│ C │▓▓│ C │▓▓│ C │▓▓│ C │▓▓ │ ← Coils cast in
    │ ▓▓▓└───┘▓▓└───┘▓▓└───┘▓▓└───┘▓▓ │   epoxy/resin
    │ ▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓ │
    └─────────────────────────────────┘

5.5 COMPONENT 5: Shaft Assembly

Function: Provides rotation axis, transfers mechanical power, supports rotor
Shaft Material Options
Material	Sources	Diameter	Suitability
Steel rod	Hardware stores, rebar	8-25mm	★★★★★
Threaded rod	Hardware stores	8-16mm	★★★★
Steel pipe	Plumbing	12-25mm	★★★
Bicycle axle	Bicycles	9-10mm	★★★★
Car axle	Vehicles	20-30mm	★★★★★
Wooden dowel	Hardware, furniture	10-25mm	★★ (temporary)

RECOMMENDED: Steel rod or threaded rod (most accessible, strongest)
Shaft Dimensioning

text

SHAFT LENGTH CALCULATION:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                        │
    │  [BEARING]   [STATOR1]   [ROTOR]   [STATOR2]   [BEARING]
    │     ║           ║          ║          ║           ║
    │  ←──╬───────────╬──────────╬──────────╬───────────╬──→
    │     ║           ║          ║          ║           ║
    │     A     B     C    D     E    F     G     H     I
    │
    │  A = Bearing width (10-20mm)
    │  B = Frame clearance (5-10mm)  
    │  C = Stator 1 thickness (10-20mm)
    │  D = Air gap 1 (3-10mm)
    │  E = Rotor thickness (15-30mm)
    │  F = Air gap 2 (3-10mm)
    │  G = Stator 2 thickness (10-20mm)
    │  H = Frame clearance (5-10mm)
    │  I = Bearing width (10-20mm)
    │  J = Drive coupling (30-50mm)
    │
    │  TOTAL = A + B + C + D + E + F + G + H + I + J + margins
    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TYPICAL TOTAL: 150-300mm depending on configuration

Shaft Preparation

text

STEP 1: Cut to Length

    Hacksaw: Mark line, cut slowly, keep straight
    Angle grinder: Faster but needs safety gear
    Pipe cutter: For pipe shafts
    
    Add 20mm extra for adjustment

STEP 2: Clean Ends

    File: Remove burrs
    Sandpaper: Smooth surface
    Wire brush: Remove rust
    
    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                         │
    │  BEFORE        AFTER                    │
    │  ╔═══╗         ╔═══╗                    │
    │  ║///║   →     ║   ║                    │
    │  ╚═══╝         ╚═══╝                    │
    │  Rough        Smooth                    │
    │                                         │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────┘

STEP 3: Mark Reference Points

    Mark these positions with marker/scratch:
    - Bearing positions
    - Rotor position
    - Drive coupling position
    
    ════════════════════════════════════════════
         │         │              │         │
         ▼         ▼              ▼         ▼
      BEARING   ROTOR          ROTOR    BEARING
       LEFT     START           END      RIGHT

Rotor-to-Shaft Connection

Method A: Set Screw (RECOMMENDED)

text

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │        ┌─────────────────┐            │
    │        │     ROTOR       │            │
    │        │    ╔═════╗      │            │
    │  ●────→│────║SHAFT║──────│            │ ← Set screw
    │        │    ╚═════╝      │            │   tightens
    │        │                 │            │   against shaft
    │        └─────────────────┘            │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘

Construction:
1. Drill hole in rotor hub slightly larger than shaft
2. Drill and tap perpendicular hole for set screw
3. Insert shaft, tighten set screw
4. Add second set screw 90° offset for security

Method B: Keyway (STRONG)

text

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │              ROTOR                    │
    │           ┌────────┐                  │
    │           │  ╔══╗  │                  │
    │           │  ║██║  │ ← Key (square    │
    │           │  ║  ║  │   steel/wood)    │
    │           │  ╚══╝  │                  │
    │           └────────┘                  │
    │              ║  ║                     │
    │              ╚══╝                     │
    │             SHAFT                     │
    │       (with keyway slot)              │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘

Construction:
1. File/grind flat on shaft (keyway)
2. Cut matching slot in rotor hole
3. Insert matching key (steel strip, hardwood)
4. Secure with collar or nut

Method C: Press Fit + Pin (SIMPLE)

text

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │        ┌─────────────────┐            │
    │        │     ROTOR       │            │
    │        │   ╔═══════╗     │            │
    │  ●─────│───╬═══════╬─────│─────●      │ ← Pin through
    │        │   ╚═══════╝     │            │   rotor AND shaft
    │        │                 │            │
    │        └─────────────────┘            │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘

Construction:
1. Drill rotor hole exact shaft size (tight fit)
2. Force/hammer shaft through
3. Drill cross hole through rotor + shaft
4. Insert pin (nail, bolt)

5.6 COMPONENT 6: Frame

Function: Holds all components in precise alignment, provides mounting
Frame Design Options

Option A: H-Frame (SIMPLE, RECOMMENDED)

text

                    TOP VIEW

    │                                     │
    │  ╔═══════════════════════════════╗  │

    │  ║    │                    │     ║  │

    │  ║    │     │ ROTOR │     │     ║  │
    │  ║    │     └───────┘     │     ║  │
    │  ║    │                    │     ║  │
    │  ║    ●                    ●     ║  │ ← Bearing supports
    │  ║                               ║  │

text

                    ISOMETRIC VIEW
    
              ┌─────────────────────┐
             ╱│                     │╲
            ╱ │                     │ ╲
           ╱  │                     │  ╲
          ╱   │    ┌─────────┐      │   ╲
         ╱    │    │  ROTOR  │      │    ╲

        │               │                 │
        └───────────────┴─────────────────┘

Option C: Plate Frame (COMPACT)

text

                    EXPLODED VIEW
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ← End plate (bearing)
        │            ○                │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
                     ↓
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ← Stator plate
        │    ╭───────────────────╮    │
        │    │   COILS           │    │
        │    ╰───────────────────╯    │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
                     ↓
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ← Rotor (spins on shaft)
        │         MAGNETS             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
                     ↓
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ← Stator plate
        │    ╭───────────────────╮    │
        │    │   COILS           │    │
        │    ╰───────────────────╯    │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
                     ↓
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐ ← End plate (bearing)
        │            ○                │
        └─────────────────────────────┘

Frame Construction (H-Frame - Detailed)

Materials Needed:

    2× Side plates (plywood 15-20mm, 200×300mm minimum)
    4× Spacer rods (threaded rod, pipe, or wood dowels)
    8× Nuts and washers (or wood screws)
    2× Bearing mounts (integrated into side plates)

text

STEP 1: Cut Side Plates

    ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                 │
    │  ○                         ○    │ ← Spacer holes
    │                                 │    (corners)
    │                                 │
    │           ◎                     │ ← Bearing hole
    │        (center)                 │
    │                                 │
    │                                 │
    │  ○                         ○    │
    │                                 │
    └─────────────────────────────────┘
    
    Mark and cut TWO identical plates
    Ensure bearing holes are perfectly aligned

STEP 2: Drill Holes

    Bearing hole: Match bearing outer diameter (tight fit)
    Spacer holes: Match spacer rod diameter
    
    ALIGNMENT CRITICAL:
    - Stack both plates
    - Clamp together
    - Drill through both simultaneously
    - This ensures alignment

STEP 3: Install Bearings

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │              PLATE                    │
    │           ┌─────────┐                 │
    │           │ BEARING │                 │
    │           │   ┌─┐   │                 │
    │           │   │█│ ← Shaft             │
    │           │   └─┘   │                 │
    │           └─────────┘                 │
    │                                       │
    │    Press bearing into hole            │
    │    Should be tight fit                │
    │    Add retaining ring or glue if loose│
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘

STEP 4: Assemble Frame

                    ASSEMBLY ORDER:
    
    1. Insert spacer rods into first plate
    
       ┌─────────────────────┐
       │ ○               ○   │
       │  ║               ║  │
       │  ║               ║  │ ← Rods standing up
       │  ║       ◎       ║  │
       │  ║               ║  │
       │  ║               ║  │
       │ ○               ○   │
       └─────────────────────┘
    
    2. Install stators and rotor on shaft
    
    3. Insert shaft through first bearing
    
    4. Lower second plate onto spacer rods
    
    5. Insert shaft into second bearing
    
    6. Secure spacer rods with nuts
    
    7. Adjust air gaps (critical!)

Air Gap Adjustment

text

AIR GAP CRITICAL DIMENSION:

    ┌─────────┐     ┌─────────┐
    │ STATOR  │     │  ROTOR  │
    │         │     │         │
    │  [COIL] │ ←─→ │ [MAG]   │
    │         │  ↑  │         │
    │         │  │  │         │
    └─────────┘  │  └─────────┘
                 │
              AIR GAP
             (3-10mm)

    TOO SMALL (< 2mm):
    - Rotor may hit stator
    - Requires perfect alignment
    - Risk of damage
    
    TOO LARGE (> 15mm):
    - Reduced magnetic coupling
    - Lower output voltage
    - Lower efficiency
    
    OPTIMAL (3-8mm):
    - Good coupling
    - Safe clearance
    - Tolerates imprecision

ADJUSTMENT METHOD:

    1. Loosen frame bolts slightly
    
    2. Insert spacers between stator and rotor:
       - Cardboard
       - Folded paper
       - Washers
       - Coins
       (Same thickness all around)
    
    3. Push stator against spacers
    
    4. Tighten frame bolts
    
    5. Remove spacers
    
    6. Rotate by hand - should spin freely
    
    7. If rubbing: increase gap
       If spinning freely: gap is correct

CHAPTER 6: ELECTRICAL SYSTEMS
6.1 Understanding Generator Output

text

RAW GENERATOR OUTPUT:

    The generator produces Alternating Current (AC)
    
    Voltage waveform:
    
         ╭─╮     ╭─╮     ╭─╮
        ╱   ╲   ╱   ╲   ╱   ╲
    ───╱─────╲─╱─────╲─╱─────╲───  → Time
       ╲     ╱ ╲     ╱ ╲     ╱
        ╲   ╱   ╲   ╱   ╲   ╱
         ╰─╯     ╰─╯     ╰─╯
    
    - Voltage swings positive AND negative
    - Frequency depends on rotation speed and pole count
    - Cannot charge batteries directly (needs rectification)
    
    FREQUENCY CALCULATION:
    
    Frequency (Hz) = (RPM × Number of Pole Pairs) / 60
    
    Example: 300 RPM, 4 pole pairs (8 magnets)
    Frequency = (300 × 4) / 60 = 20 Hz

6.2 Rectification (AC → DC)

Purpose: Convert alternating current to direct current for batteries/devices
Single-Phase Full Bridge Rectifier

text

CIRCUIT DIAGRAM:

              AC INPUT
                 │
    ─────────────┼─────────────
    │            │            │
    │     ┌──────┴──────┐     │
    │     │             │     │
    ▼     │             │     ▼
   ─┬─    │             │    ─┬─
    │ D1  │             │ D2  │
    │     │             │     │
    ├─────┼──── DC+ ────┼─────┤
    │     │             │     │
    │ D3  │             │ D4  │
    │     │             │     │
   ─┴─    │             │    ─┴─
    ▲     │             │     ▲
    │     │             │     │
    │     └──────┬──────┘     │
    │            │            │
    ─────────────┼─────────────
                 │
              DC─ (GND)

COMPONENTS:
    D1-D4: Diodes (1N4001-1N4007 or equivalent)
           Rating: At least 1A, 50V minimum
           
    Sources for diodes:
    - Old phone chargers
    - Power supplies
    - LED drivers
    - Any electronics with AC input

CONSTRUCTION:

    Simple Layout:
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │     AC ●────┬────[D1]────┬──── DC+    │
    │             │            │            │
    │             │            │            │
    │          [D3]          [D2]           │
    │             │            │            │
    │             │            │            │
    │     AC ●────┴────[D4]────┴──── DC-    │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    Point-to-point wiring on scrap wood or cardboard
    Solder or twist connections
    Insulate all joints with tape

Three-Phase Rectifier (6 Diodes)

text

THREE-PHASE FULL BRIDGE:

    Phase A ─────[D1]────┬─── DC+
                         │
    Phase B ─────[D2]────┤
                         │
    Phase C ─────[D3]────┤
                         │
    Phase A ─────[D4]────┤
                         │
    Phase B ─────[D5]────┼─── DC-
                         │
    Phase C ─────[D6]────┘

SIMPLIFIED SCHEMATIC:

              ┌──[D1]──┐
    Phase A ──┤        ├──┐
              └──[D4]──┘  │
                          │
              ┌──[D2]──┐  │
    Phase B ──┤        ├──┼── DC Output
              └──[D5]──┘  │
                          │
              ┌──[D3]──┐  │
    Phase C ──┤        ├──┘
              └──[D6]──┘

ADVANTAGE: Much smoother DC output (less ripple)

6.3 Filtering (Smoothing)

text

UNFILTERED DC:

    ───╭─╮─╭─╮─╭─╮─╭─╮─╭─╮───  ← Pulsating DC (ripple)
       │ │ │ │ │ │ │ │ │ │
    ───┴─┴─┴─┴─┴─┴─┴─┴─┴─┴───

FILTERED DC:

    ──────────────────────────  ← Smooth DC
    
PURPOSE: Smooth pulsating DC into steady DC

FILTER CIRCUIT:

    Rectifier Output                    Filtered Output
          │                                   │
          ├──────────┬──────────┬─────────────┤
          │          │          │             │
          │       ───┴───    ───┴───          │
          │       ───┬───    ───┬───          │
          │  CAP 1   │  CAP 2   │             │
          │          │          │             │
          └──────────┴──────────┴─────────────┘
                          │
                         GND

CAPACITOR SIZING:

    Capacitance = Current (A) / (Ripple × Frequency)
    
    Example: 1A output, 10% ripple, 60Hz
    C = 1 / (0.1 × 60) = 0.167F = 167,000µF
    
    Practical: Use largest capacitors available
    Typical: 1000-10,000µF per amp
    
CAPACITOR SOURCES:
    - Computer power supplies (large electrolytics)
    - Old amplifiers
    - Motor start capacitors
    - Camera flash units

⚠️ WARNING: Large capacitors store dangerous energy
    - Always discharge before handling
    - Connect resistor across terminals before working
    - Observe polarity (+ and - marked)

6.4 Voltage Regulation

text

PURPOSE: Maintain constant output voltage regardless of speed/load

OPTION A: Zener Diode Shunt (SIMPLE)

          ┌────────────────────────────────┐
          │                                │
    IN+ ──┼────────────[R]─────┬─────────── OUT+
          │                    │           
          │                  ──┴──         
          │                  ZENER         
          │                  ──┬──         
          │                    │           
    IN- ──┼────────────────────┴─────────── OUT-
          │                                │
          └────────────────────────────────┘
    
    R = (V_in - V_out) / I_zener
    
    Zener voltage = desired output voltage
    Wastes excess power as heat
    Simple but inefficient

OPTION B: Linear Regulator IC (RECOMMENDED)

    Common regulators (scavenge from any power supply):
    
    │ Part Number │ Output Voltage │ Max Current │
    │ 7805        │ 5V            │ 1A          │
    │ 7806        │ 6V            │ 1A          │
    │ 7809        │ 9V            │ 1A          │
    │ 7812        │ 12V           │ 1A          │
    │ LM317       │ Adjustable    │ 1.5A        │

    CONNECTION:
    
              ┌──────────┐
    IN+ ──────┤ IN  OUT  ├────── OUT+
              │          │
              │   GND    │
              └────┬─────┘
                   │
    IN- ──────────┴──────────── OUT-
    
    Requires: Input voltage > Output voltage + 2V
    Add heat sink for currents > 0.3A

OPTION C: Buck Converter Module (BEST)

    Scavenge from:
    - USB car chargers
    - Phone chargers
    - Solar charge controllers
    - LED drivers
    
    Already includes:
    - Voltage regulation
    - Current limiting
    - Efficiency 80-95%
    
    Simply connect input and output

6.5 Complete Electrical Schematic

text

COMPLETE SINGLE-PHASE SYSTEM:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   GENERATOR          RECTIFIER         FILTER    REGULATOR  │
    │                                                             │
    │   ┌───────┐        ┌─────────┐      ┌──────┐    ┌──────┐   │
    │   │ ~     │   AC   │  D1-D4  │  DC  │ CAP  │    │ REG  │   │
    │   │   ∿   │───────→│  BRIDGE │─────→│ 1000 │───→│ 7805 │──→│ 5V OUT
    │   │ ~     │        │         │      │  µF  │    │      │   │
    │   └───────┘        └─────────┘      └──────┘    └──────┘   │
    │       │                 │               │           │       │
    │       └─────────────────┴───────────────┴───────────┘       │
    │                              GND                            │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

COMPLETE THREE-PHASE SYSTEM:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   GENERATOR          RECTIFIER         FILTER    OUTPUT     │
    │                                                             │
    │   ┌───────┐        ┌─────────┐      ┌──────┐               │
    │   │ A ~   │────────│  D1     │──┐   │      │               │
    │   │       │        │    D4   │  │   │ CAP  │               │
    │   │ B ~   │────────│  D2     │──┼──→│ 2200 │──→ DC OUT     │
    │   │       │        │    D5   │  │   │  µF  │               │
    │   │ C ~   │────────│  D3     │──┘   │      │               │
    │   │       │        │    D6   │      │      │               │
    │   └───────┘        └─────────┘      └──────┘               │
    │                         │               │                   │
    │                         └───────────────┘                   │
    │                              GND                            │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

6.6 Protection Circuits

text

ESSENTIAL PROTECTIONS:

1. FUSE (REQUIRED)

    Generator ──────[FUSE]────── Rectifier
    
    Rating: 125-150% of expected max current
    Sources: Automotive fuses, electronics
    
    Protects against:
    - Short circuits
    - Overcurrent
    - Wiring faults

2. REVERSE POLARITY PROTECTION

    ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                  │
    │  IN+ ────[DIODE]────── OUT+      │
    │                                  │
    │  IN- ───────────────── OUT-      │
    │                                  │
    └──────────────────────────────────┘
    
    Prevents damage if connections reversed
    Use diode rated for full current

3. OVERVOLTAGE PROTECTION (TVS/MOV)

    ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                  │
    │  + ──────────┬─────────── OUT+   │
    │              │                   │
    │            [TVS]                 │
    │              │                   │
    │  - ──────────┴─────────── OUT-   │
    │                                  │
    └──────────────────────────────────┘
    
    TVS = Transient Voltage Suppressor
    MOV = Metal Oxide Varistor
    Clamps voltage spikes
    Sources: Surge protectors, power strips

4. LED INDICATOR

    + ────[R]────[LED]──── -
    
    R = (Voltage - 2) / 0.010  (for 10mA LED current)
    
    Example: 12V system
    R = (12 - 2) / 0.01 = 1000Ω = 1kΩ
    
    Provides visual confirmation of power

CHAPTER 7: COMPLETE ASSEMBLY
7.1 Assembly Sequence

text

PHASE 1: SUBASSEMBLY PREPARATION

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                     │
    │  □ Rotor disc cut and drilled                      │
    │  □ Magnets mounted on rotor (alternating poles)    │
    │  □ Magnets secured (epoxy cured / mechanically)    │
    │  □ Stator rings cut                                │
    │  □ Coils wound (all identical turns)               │
    │  □ Coils mounted on stators                        │
    │  □ Coils connected (series/parallel/3-phase)       │
    │  □ Connection leads extended                        │
    │  □ Frame pieces cut                                │
    │  □ Bearing holes drilled                           │
    │  □ Bearings installed                              │
    │  □ Shaft cut and prepared                          │
    │                                                     │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PHASE 2: MECHANICAL ASSEMBLY

    Step 1: Install Shaft in Rotor
    ────────────────────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │                             │
        │    Insert shaft through     │
        │    rotor center hole        │
        │                             │
        │        ┌───────┐            │
        │        │███████│ ← Rotor    │
        │        │██ █ ██│            │
        │        │██ █ ██│ ← Shaft    │
        │        │███████│            │
        │        └───────┘            │
        │                             │
        │    Secure with set screw,   │
        │    key, or pin              │
        │                             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 2: Install First Frame Plate
    ──────────────────────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │                             │
        │    Insert shaft through     │
        │    bearing in first plate   │
        │                             │
        │    ═══════════════════      │
        │         ║                   │
        │         ║ ← Shaft           │
        │         ║                   │
        │    ┌────╫────┐              │
        │    │████║████│              │
        │    │████║████│ ← Rotor      │
        │    └────╫────┘              │
        │         ║                   │
        │                             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 3: Position Stators
    ────────────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │                             │
        │    Stator 1 on spacers      │
        │    (or threaded rods)       │
        │                             │
        │    ═══════════════════      │
        │    │ ╭─────────╮   │        │
        │    │ │ STATOR1 │   │        │
        │    │ ╰─────────╯   │        │
        │    │      │        │        │
        │    │   [ROTOR]     │        │
        │    │      │        │        │
        │    │ ╭─────────╮   │        │
        │    │ │ STATOR2 │   │        │
        │    │ ╰─────────╯   │        │
        │    ═══════════════════      │
        │                             │
        │    Gap spacers in place     │
        │    (cardboard/washers)      │
        │                             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 4: Install Second Frame Plate
    ───────────────────────────────────
    
        Insert shaft through second bearing
        Tighten all spacer bolts
        
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │                             │
        │    ═══════════════════      │ ← Plate 2
        │    │   ┌─────────┐   │      │
        │    │   │ STATOR2 │   │      │
        │    │   └─────────┘   │      │
        │    │   ┌─────────┐   │      │
        │    │   │  ROTOR  │   │      │
        │    │   └─────────┘   │      │
        │    │   ┌─────────┐   │      │
        │    │   │ STATOR1 │   │      │
        │    │   └─────────┘   │      │
        │    ═══════════════════      │ ← Plate 1
        │                             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 5: Remove Gap Spacers
    ──────────────────────────
    
        Remove cardboard/washers
        Rotate shaft by hand
        Must spin freely
        
        If rubbing: loosen, readjust, retighten

PHASE 3: ELECTRICAL ASSEMBLY

    Step 6: Route Wires
    ───────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────┐
        │                             │
        │    Route coil leads out     │
        │    of frame through         │
        │    holes or slots           │
        │                             │
        │    ════════════════════     │
        │    │             ────│───→ To rectifier
        │    │   GENERATOR    │      │
        │    │             ────│───→ To rectifier
        │    ════════════════════     │
        │                             │
        │    Secure wires so they     │
        │    cannot contact rotor     │
        │                             │
        └─────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 7: Connect Rectifier
    ─────────────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
        │                                     │
        │    GENERATOR        RECTIFIER       │
        │    ┌───────┐       ┌─────────┐      │
        │    │   AC  │───────│  DIODE  │      │
        │    │   AC  │───────│  BRIDGE │      │
        │    └───────┘       └────┬────┘      │
        │                         │           │
        │                    DC+ ─┴─ DC-      │
        │                                     │
        └─────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 8: Add Filter and Regulator
    ─────────────────────────────────
    
        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │                                                 │
        │    RECTIFIER      CAPACITOR      REGULATOR      │
        │    ┌───────┐      ┌──────┐       ┌──────┐       │
        │    │       │      │      │       │      │       │
        │ ───│  DC+  │──┬───│ 1000 │───┬───│ IN   │─── +  │
        │    │       │  │   │  µF  │   │   │      │       │
        │    │       │  │   │      │   │   │ OUT  │─── +OUT
        │    │       │  │   └──────┘   │   │      │       │
        │ ───│  DC-  │──┴──────────────┴───│ GND  │─── -  │
        │    │       │                     │      │       │
        │    └───────┘                     └──────┘       │
        │                                                 │
        └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    Step 9: Add Protection
    ──────────────────────
    
        Add fuse in positive line
        Add LED indicator
        Insulate all connections

7.2 Drive Coupling

Purpose: Connect external power source (hand, wind, water, motor) to generator shaft
Hand Crank

text

SIMPLE CRANK:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                             │
    │         HANDLE                              │
    │           │                                 │
    │           ●──────── ARM (200-300mm)         │
    │                      │                      │
    │                      │                      │
    │                      ●──── CONNECTION       │
    │                      │     TO SHAFT         │
    │                   ┌──┴──┐                   │
    │                   │SHAFT│                   │
    │                   │     │                   │
    │                   └─────┘                   │
    │                                             │
    │    CONSTRUCTION:                            │
    │    - Arm: Metal strip, wood, pipe           │
    │    - Handle: Piece of pipe/wood (rotates)   │
    │    - Connection: Bolt through arm + shaft   │
    │      or welded/epoxied                      │
    │                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    Arm length determines mechanical advantage:
    - Longer arm = easier to turn, slower RPM
    - Shorter arm = harder to turn, faster RPM
    
    Recommended: 150-250mm arm length

Pulley/Belt Drive

text

BELT DRIVE SYSTEM:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                             │
    │                                             │
    │    DRIVE         BELT         GENERATOR     │
    │    PULLEY                     PULLEY        │
    │                                             │
    │    ╭─────╮                   ╭───╮          │
    │    │     │═══════════════════│   │          │
    │    │     │                   │   │          │
    │    │     │═══════════════════│   │          │
    │    ╰─────╯                   ╰───╯          │
    │      ↑                         ↑            │
    │    Large                     Small          │
    │    (slow)                   (fast)          │
    │                                             │
    │    SPEED RATIO:                             │
    │    Generator RPM = Drive RPM × (D1 / D2)    │
    │    D1 = Drive pulley diameter               │
    │    D2 = Generator pulley diameter           │
    │                                             │
    │    Example: 60 RPM pedal × (200mm/50mm)     │
    │           = 240 RPM at generator            │
    │                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

BELT SOURCES:
    - Bicycle inner tubes (cut)
    - Rubber bands (multiple)
    - Sewing machine belts
    - Vacuum cleaner belts
    - Automotive belts (V-belt)
    - Rope (friction wrap)

PULLEY SOURCES/MAKING:
    - Wheels (skateboard, inline skate)
    - Bottle caps stacked
    - Cut plywood discs with groove
    - Cans (coffee cans, paint cans)

Direct Coupling

text

DIRECT SHAFT COUPLING:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                             │
    │     DRIVE SHAFT        GENERATOR SHAFT      │
    │         │                    │              │
    │         ▼                    ▼              │
    │     ════╪════════════════════╪════          │
    │         │    COUPLING        │              │
    │         │   ┌────────┐       │              │
    │         └───│ SLEEVE │───────┘              │
    │             └────────┘                      │
    │                                             │
    │    COUPLING METHODS:                        │
    │                                             │
    │    A. Sleeve + Set Screws                   │
    │       ┌────────────────────────┐            │
    │       │ ●    ║████████║    ● │            │
    │       └────────────────────────┘            │
    │       Pipe sleeve over both shafts          │
    │       Set screws lock each shaft            │
    │                                             │
    │    B. Hose Clamp Coupling                   │
    │       ┌────────────────────────┐            │
    │       │ ○    ║████████║    ○ │            │
    │       └────────────────────────┘            │
    │       Rubber hose over shafts               │
    │       Hose clamps on each end               │
    │       (Allows slight misalignment)          │
    │                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CHAPTER 8: TESTING & COMMISSIONING
8.1 Pre-Power Testing

text

MECHANICAL CHECKS:

□ Rotor spins freely by hand
  └─ If not: Check air gap, bearing alignment

□ No rubbing sounds
  └─ If rubbing: Increase air gap

□ Shaft centered in both bearings
  └─ If off-center: Realign frame

□ All fasteners tight
  └─ Check: Set screws, frame bolts, bearing retainers

□ Magnets secure
  └─ If loose: Re-glue, add mechanical retention

□ Wires clear of rotating parts
  └─ If close: Reroute, add insulation

ELECTRICAL CHECKS:

□ Coil resistance measured
  └─ Record value: _____ Ω per coil
  └─ All coils should be similar (±10%)

□ No shorts between coils
  └─ Measure resistance between phases: should be open

□ No short to frame/shaft
  └─ Measure coil to frame: should be infinite (open)

□ Connections secure
  └─ Tug test all connections

□ Rectifier diodes tested
  └─ Forward: ~0.6V drop
  └─ Reverse: Open (no reading)

8.2 First Power Test

text

PROCEDURE:

STEP 1: Connect Multimeter
─────────────────────────

    Set to AC Voltage
    Connect to generator output (before rectifier)
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │    GENERATOR ────┬───── MULTIMETER    │
    │                  │                    │
    │    (AC output)   │      (AC V mode)   │
    │                  │                    │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘

STEP 2: Rotate by Hand (Slow)
─────────────────────────────

    Rotate shaft slowly (~60 RPM)
    
    EXPECTED:
    - Multimeter shows AC voltage
    - Voltage increases with speed
    
    IF NO VOLTAGE:
    - Check coil connections
    - Verify alternating magnet polarity
    - Check for broken wires

STEP 3: Increase Speed Gradually
────────────────────────────────

    Record voltage at different speeds:
    
    │ Speed (RPM)  │ AC Voltage (V) │
    │──────────────│────────────────│
    │     60       │                │
    │    120       │                │
    │    180       │                │
    │    240       │                │
    │    300       │                │
    
    Voltage should increase approximately linearly

STEP 4: Test DC Output
──────────────────────

    Connect multimeter to rectifier output
    Set to DC Voltage
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │    GEN → RECTIFIER ────── MULTIMETER  │
    │                   ↓                   │
    │                (DC out)  (DC V mode)  │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    DC voltage should be ~1.4× AC voltage (full bridge)
    
    Example: 10V AC → ~14V DC

STEP 5: Load Test
─────────────────

    Connect small load (LED with resistor, small bulb)
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                       │
    │    GEN → RECT → [LED + 1kΩ]           │
    │                                       │
    │    Rotate generator                   │
    │    LED should light                   │
    │                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    If LED lights: Generator working
    If LED doesn't light:
    - Check LED polarity
    - Check connections
    - Increase speed

8.3 Performance Measurement

text

MEASURING OUTPUT POWER:

    Power (Watts) = Voltage × Current
    
PROCEDURE:

    1. Set up with load resistor of known value
    
       GEN → RECT → [LOAD RESISTOR] → GND
                    └── V meter across resistor
                    └── A meter in series
    
    2. Measure voltage and current at steady speed
    
    3. Calculate power: P = V × I
    
    4. Record results:
    
    │ Speed (RPM) │ Voltage (V) │ Current (A) │ Power (W) │
    │─────────────│─────────────│─────────────│───────────│
    │             │             │             │           │
    │             │             │             │           │
    │             │             │             │           │

EFFICIENCY MEASUREMENT:

    1. Measure mechanical input power:
       P_in = Torque × Angular velocity
       
       (Simplified: P_in ≈ Force × Arm length × RPM × 0.105)
    
    2. Measure electrical output power:
       P_out = V × I
    
    3. Calculate efficiency:
       Efficiency = (P_out / P_in) × 100%
    
    EXPECTED: 50-70% for DIY construction

8.4 Performance Optimization

text

IF OUTPUT IS LOW:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Problem               │ Solution                   │
    │───────────────────────│────────────────────────────│
    │ Voltage too low       │ More coil turns            │
    │                       │ Stronger magnets           │
    │                       │ Faster rotation            │
    │                       │ Smaller air gap            │
    │───────────────────────│────────────────────────────│
    │ Current too low       │ Thicker wire               │
    │                       │ Parallel coil connection   │
    │                       │ More copper (less res.)    │
    │───────────────────────│────────────────────────────│
    │ Excessive heating     │ Thicker wire               │
    │                       │ Better ventilation         │
    │                       │ Reduce current draw        │
    │───────────────────────│────────────────────────────│
    │ Mechanical drag       │ Better bearings            │
    │                       │ Alignment                  │
    │                       │ Lubrication                │
    │───────────────────────│────────────────────────────│
    │ Vibration             │ Balance rotor              │
    │                       │ Secure frame               │
    │                       │ Check bearing fit          │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CHAPTER 9: TROUBLESHOOTING
9.1 Diagnostic Flowchart

text

START: Generator not producing expected output
│
├── Does rotor spin freely?
│   ├── NO → Check air gap, bearings, alignment
│   │         ↓
│   │         Fix mechanical issue, retest
│   │
│   └── YES → Continue
│              │
├── Is there AC voltage output?
│   ├── NO → Check magnet polarity (must alternate)
│   │         Check coil continuity (should have resistance)
│   │         Check coil connections
│   │         ↓
│   │         If still no output: Rewire/rebuild coils
│   │
│   └── YES → Continue
│              │
├── Is voltage increasing with speed?
│   ├── NO → Check for shorted turns in coils
│   │         Check magnet arrangement
│   │
│   └── YES → Continue
│              │
├── Is there DC voltage after rectifier?
│   ├── NO → Test diodes individually
│   │         Check rectifier wiring
│   │         Replace failed diodes
│   │
│   └── YES → Continue
│              │
├── Does voltage drop significantly under load?
│   ├── YES → Excessive coil resistance (thinner wire)
│   │          Weak magnets
│   │          Poor connections (high resistance)
│   │
│   └── NO → Generator working correctly
│            │
│            Optimize for target application
│
END

9.2 Common Problems and Solutions

text

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: No output at all                                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Broken wire in coil                                        │
│   • Coils not connected                                        │
│   • All magnets same polarity                                  │
│   • Magnets fallen off rotor                                   │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Measure each coil individually (should show resistance)   │
│   2. Verify magnet arrangement (alternating N-S-N-S)           │
│   3. Check all connections with multimeter                     │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Repair broken wire connections                             │
│   • Rearrange magnets to alternate                             │
│   • Reconnect loose wires                                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: Very low voltage                                       │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Too few coil turns                                         │
│   • Air gap too large                                          │
│   • Weak magnets (ferrite vs neodymium)                        │
│   • Slow rotation speed                                        │
│   • Shorted turns in coil                                      │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Count coil turns (record for comparison)                  │
│   2. Measure air gap (should be 3-8mm)                         │
│   3. Test magnet strength (should hold paper clips)            │
│   4. Measure coil resistance (very low = shorted)              │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Rewind coils with more turns                               │
│   • Reduce air gap (adjust stator position)                    │
│   • Add stronger magnets or more magnets                       │
│   • Increase rotation speed (gearing)                          │
│   • Rewind damaged coil                                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: High voltage but no current (dims under load)          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Wire too thin (high resistance)                            │
│   • Poor connections (added resistance)                        │
│   • Insufficient magnetic flux                                 │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Measure total coil resistance                             │
│   2. Check all connection resistances                          │
│   3. Test short-circuit current (briefly, carefully)           │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Rewind with thicker wire (lower gauge number)              │
│   • Parallel-connect existing coils instead of series          │
│   • Improve all connections (solder, crimp)                    │
│   • Add more magnets                                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: Cogging (jerky rotation, hard to start)                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Magnets attracted to metal in stator                       │
│   • Steel stator core                                          │
│   • Uneven magnet spacing                                      │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Check if stator material is magnetic (test with magnet)   │
│   2. Check magnet spacing uniformity                           │
│   3. Feel for magnetic "catches" when rotating slowly          │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Use non-magnetic stator (wood, plastic, aluminum)          │
│   • Space magnets evenly (use template)                        │
│   • Accept some cogging in iron-core designs (normal)          │
│   • Add flywheel mass for momentum                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: Excessive heating                                      │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Overloaded (drawing too much current)                      │
│   • Wire too thin for current level                            │
│   • Poor ventilation                                           │
│   • Eddy currents in metal stator                              │
│   • Short circuit in coil                                      │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Measure current draw (compare to wire rating)             │
│   2. Feel where heat is concentrated                           │
│   3. Check for burnt insulation smell                          │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Reduce load                                                │
│   • Use thicker wire                                           │
│   • Add ventilation holes, fan                                 │
│   • Use non-conductive stator                                  │
│   • Rewind shorted coil                                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PROBLEM: Noise/vibration                                        │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAUSES:                                                         │
│   • Unbalanced rotor                                           │
│   • Loose components                                           │
│   • Worn bearings                                              │
│   • Rotor hitting stator                                       │
│   • Resonance in frame                                         │
│                                                                 │
│ DIAGNOSIS:                                                      │
│   1. Spin slowly, listen for source                            │
│   2. Check for loose fasteners                                 │
│   3. Check air gap uniformity around entire rotation           │
│                                                                 │
│ FIX:                                                            │
│   • Balance rotor (add small weights opposite heavy side)      │
│   • Tighten all fasteners                                      │
│   • Replace/lubricate bearings                                 │
│   • Increase air gap, realign                                  │
│   • Stiffen frame, add damping                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PART III: SCALING TIERS
TIER 0: MINIMAL BUILD (1-5 Watts)

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    TIER 0: SURVIVAL MINIMUM
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PURPOSE: LED lighting, small radio, emergency phone charge (slow)

SPECIFICATIONS:
    Output: 1-5W at 5-12V DC
    Size: Fits in hand
    Weight: < 500g
    Build time: 1-2 hours
    
COMPONENTS:

    MAGNETS: 4× Neodymium (from 1 hard drive or 4 small speakers)
             Arrangement: N-S-N-S at 90° spacing
             
    COILS: 4× wound coils
           Wire: AWG 22-26, 60-100 turns each
           Connection: All in series
           
    ROTOR: 80-100mm diameter disc
           Material: Plywood 10mm or plastic
           
    STATORS: 2× rings, 60mm ID, 130mm OD
             Coils mounted between magnet paths
             
    BEARING: 2× 608 (skateboard bearing) or bottle cap bearing
    
    SHAFT: 8mm steel rod, 100mm length
    
    FRAME: Simple H-frame, 150mm × 150mm × 80mm

DIAGRAM:

    TOP VIEW:                    SIDE VIEW:
    
         ╭───╮                   ════════════
        ╱  N  ╲                  ║  STATOR  ║
       ╱       ╲                 ║──────────║
    ╭─S    ●    S─╮              ║  ROTOR   ║
       ╲       ╱                 ║──────────║
        ╲  N  ╱                  ║  STATOR  ║
         ╰───╯                   ════════════
    
    Rotor: 90mm Ø                Height: 50mm total

ELECTRICAL:

    ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐
    │4 COILS │────→│ BRIDGE │────→│  5V    │────→ USB OUT
    │(series)│     │ RECT   │     │ REG    │
    └────────┘     └────────┘     └────────┘
    
    Target: 8-15V AC at 150+ RPM → 5V DC regulated

PERFORMANCE:

    │ RPM     │ Open Voltage │ Loaded (5V USB) │
    │─────────│──────────────│─────────────────│
    │   60    │    3V        │ (insufficient)  │
    │  120    │    6V        │   0.2A (1W)     │
    │  180    │    9V        │   0.5A (2.5W)   │
    │  240    │   12V        │   0.8A (4W)     │
    │  300    │   15V        │   1.0A (5W)     │

MATERIALS SCAVENGING LIST:

    □ 1 old hard drive (2 magnets, 1 bearing, wire)
    □ 1 old speaker (additional magnets)
    □ 1 old transformer (copper wire)
    □ 1 broken skateboard/rollerblade (bearings)
    □ 1 broken phone charger (rectifier, USB port)
    □ Scrap wood or plastic for frame

BUILD STEPS SUMMARY:

    1. Extract magnets from hard drive (careful - strong!)
    2. Cut rotor disc (90mm circle)
    3. Mount 4 magnets at 90° intervals, alternating polarity
    4. Cut stator rings (130mm OD, 60mm ID)
    5. Wind 4 coils (80 turns each, AWG 24)
    6. Mount coils on stators at 45° offset from magnets
    7. Connect coils in series
    8. Build simple H-frame with bearing mounts
    9. Assemble: Plate → Stator → Rotor → Stator → Plate
    10. Add hand crank
    11. Wire to rectifier and USB regulator
    12. Test and adjust

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

TIER 1: BASIC BUILD (5-20 Watts)

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    TIER 1: PRACTICAL BASIC
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PURPOSE: Phone charging (normal speed), LED lighting system,
         small radio, fan

SPECIFICATIONS:
    Output: 5-20W at 12V DC
    Size: ~200mm diameter
    Weight: 1-2 kg
    Build time: 3-5 hours
    
COMPONENTS:

    MAGNETS: 8× Neodymium (from 2 hard drives or motors)
             Arrangement: N-S-N-S-N-S-N-S at 45° spacing
             Size: 20-30mm each
             
    COILS: 12× wound coils (3-phase configuration)
           Wire: AWG 18-22, 80-150 turns each
           Connection: 3-phase star (4 coils per phase)
           
    ROTOR: 150mm diameter disc
           Material: 15mm plywood or HDPE
           
    STATORS: 2× rings, 100mm ID, 200mm OD
             
    BEARINGS: 2× 608 ball bearings
    
    SHAFT: 10mm steel rod, 150mm length
    
    FRAME: H-frame, 250mm × 250mm × 100mm

DIAGRAM:

    TOP VIEW (ROTOR):
    
              ╭─────╮
            ╱ N     S ╲
          ╱             ╲
        ╭S      ●        N╮
        │                 │
        ╰N               S╯
          ╲             ╱
            ╲ S     N ╱
              ╰─────╯
    
    8 magnets at 45° spacing
    Alternating N-S pattern

    TOP VIEW (STATOR - one side):
    
              ╭─────╮
            ╱ [C1]   [C2] ╲
          ╱                 ╲
        [C12]     ○      [C3]
        │                   │
        [C11]             [C4]
          ╲                 ╱
            ╲ [C10] [C5] ╱
              ╰─────╯
         [C9] [C8] [C7] [C6]
    
    12 coils at 30° spacing

THREE-PHASE WIRING:

    Phase A: C1 → C4 → C7 → C10 (in series)
    Phase B: C2 → C5 → C8 → C11 (in series)
    Phase C: C3 → C6 → C9 → C12 (in series)
    
    Connect as star:
    
            Phase A ────────────┐
                                │
            Phase B ────────────┼─── Star Point (optional neutral)
                                │
            Phase C ────────────┘
    
    Feed to 6-diode three-phase rectifier

ELECTRICAL:

    ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐
    │3-PHASE │────→│6-DIODE │────→│ 2200µF │────→│  12V   │────→ OUT
    │  GEN   │     │ BRIDGE │     │  CAP   │     │  REG   │
    └────────┘     └────────┘     └────────┘     └────────┘

PERFORMANCE:

    │ RPM     │ Open Voltage │ At 12V Load     │
    │─────────│──────────────│─────────────────│
    │  100    │    8V        │ (insufficient)  │
    │  150    │   12V        │   0.3A (3.6W)   │
    │  200    │   16V        │   0.6A (7.2W)   │
    │  250    │   20V        │   1.0A (12W)    │
    │  300    │   24V        │   1.5A (18W)    │

DRIVE OPTIONS:

    A. Hand crank (150-250 RPM comfortable)
    B. Pedal drive via bicycle wheel (200-400 RPM)
    C. Small wind turbine blade (depends on wind)
    D. Small water wheel (depends on flow)
