TIER 2: STANDARD BUILD (20-100 Watts)

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    TIER 2: HOUSEHOLD STANDARD
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PURPOSE: Laptop charging, multiple phones, LED lighting for room,
         small fan, small TV, WiFi router

SPECIFICATIONS:
    Output: 20-100W at 12V or 24V DC
    Size: ~300mm diameter
    Weight: 3-6 kg
    Build time: 6-10 hours
    
COMPONENTS:

    MAGNETS: 16× Neodymium (from 4+ hard drives, motors, speakers)
             Arrangement: Alternating at 22.5° spacing
             Size: 25-40mm each
             
    COILS: 12× or 24× wound coils
           Wire: AWG 16-20, 100-200 turns each
           Connection: 3-phase star or delta
           
    ROTOR: 250mm diameter disc
           Material: 18mm plywood, HDPE, or aluminum
           
    STATORS: 2× rings, 160mm ID, 320mm OD
             
    BEARINGS: 2× 6001 or 6202 ball bearings (or 2× 608 doubled)
    
    SHAFT: 12-16mm steel rod, 200mm length
    
    FRAME: Reinforced H-frame or box frame

DUAL ROTOR OPTION (Higher Power):

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                       │
    │   STATOR    ROTOR1    STATOR    ROTOR2    STATOR     │
    │   ═════     ═════     ═════     ═════     ═════      │
    │   ║   ║     ║ M ║     ║ C ║     ║ M ║     ║   ║      │
    │   ║ C ║←───→║   ║←───→║   ║←───→║   ║←───→║ C ║      │
    │   ║   ║     ║ M ║     ║ C ║     ║ M ║     ║   ║      │
    │   ═════     ═════     ═════     ═════     ═════      │
    │                                                       │
    │   Doubles power output for same diameter              │
    │   Rotors on same shaft, magnets aligned               │
    │                                                       │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PERFORMANCE (Single Rotor, 16 magnets, 12 coils):

    │ RPM     │ Open Voltage │ At 24V Load     │
    │─────────│──────────────│─────────────────│
    │  150    │   12V        │ (insufficient)  │
    │  200    │   18V        │ (insufficient)  │
    │  250    │   24V        │   0.8A (19W)    │
    │  300    │   30V        │   1.5A (36W)    │
    │  400    │   40V        │   2.5A (60W)    │
    │  500    │   50V        │   3.5A (84W)    │

DRIVE OPTIONS:

    A. Pedal generator (bicycle frame)
       - 200-400 RPM from 60-80 pedal RPM with 3:1-5:1 gearing
       - Sustained 50-100W output
       
    B. Wind turbine
       - 3-blade, 1-2m diameter rotor
       - ~100W at 8-10 m/s wind
       
    C. Water wheel/turbine
       - Pelton or turgo for high head
       - Undershot/overshot for low head
       - Output varies with water flow

CHARGING SYSTEM:

    ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐     ┌────────┐
    │3-PHASE │────→│6-DIODE │────→│ FILTER │────→│ CHARGE │
    │  GEN   │     │ BRIDGE │     │4700µF  │     │CONTROL │
    └────────┘     └────────┘     └────────┘     └────────┘
                                                      │
                                                      ▼
                                               ┌────────────┐
                                               │12V BATTERY │
                                               │  (or 24V)  │
                                               └────────────┘

        Charge controller prevents:
    - Overcharging (battery damage, gas release)
    - Deep discharge (battery damage)
    - Reverse current (battery draining through generator)
    
    DIY CHARGE CONTROLLER (Simple):
    
    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   FROM         BLOCKING      SHUNT         TO               │
    │   RECTIFIER    DIODE         REGULATOR     BATTERY          │
    │                                                             │
    │   ────●────[D1]────●────────●──────────────● (+)            │
    │                    │        │                               │
    │                    │     [RELAY]                            │
    │                    │        │                               │
    │                 [LED]    [LOAD] ← Dump load (resistor/bulb) │
    │                    │        │                               │
    │   ─────────────────┴────────┴──────────────● (-)            │
    │                                                             │
    │   OPERATION:                                                │
    │   - D1 prevents reverse current                            │
    │   - LED shows charging                                      │
    │   - When battery reaches 14.4V (12V system):               │
    │     Relay activates, diverts power to dump load            │
    │   - When battery drops to 13.8V:                           │
    │     Relay deactivates, charging resumes                    │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    SCAVENGED CHARGE CONTROLLER:
    
    Best source: Solar charge controllers
    - Already designed for this purpose
    - Available from solar systems, camping gear
    - Connect directly: Gen DC+ → Controller IN+
                        Gen DC- → Controller IN-
    - Controller handles battery connection

COMPLETE TIER 2 PARTS LIST:

    MECHANICAL:
    □ 1× Rotor disc 250mm Ø × 18mm
    □ 2× Stator rings 320mm OD × 160mm ID × 15mm
    □ 2× Frame plates 350mm × 350mm × 18mm
    □ 4× Spacer rods M10 × 120mm (or wood dowels)
    □ 2× Bearings (6001 or similar)
    □ 1× Shaft 14mm × 200mm
    □ Assorted fasteners, screws, bolts
    
    MAGNETS:
    □ 16× Neodymium magnets 30mm × 10mm × 5mm (or equivalent)
    
    ELECTRICAL:
    □ Copper wire AWG 18 (~50m for 12 coils × 150 turns)
    □ 6× Diodes 1N5408 (3A) or similar
    □ 2× Capacitors 2200µF 50V
    □ 1× Charge controller (or components for DIY)
    □ 1× 12V or 24V battery (deep cycle recommended)
    □ Wire, connectors, fuse holders, fuses
    
    TOOLS RECOMMENDED:
    □ Jigsaw or circular saw
    □ Drill with bits
    □ Soldering iron
    □ Multimeter
    □ Wrenches, screwdrivers
    □ Clamps

TIER 3: ADVANCED BUILD (100-500 Watts)

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    TIER 3: ADVANCED HIGH POWER
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PURPOSE: Significant household load, power tools, refrigerator,
         washing machine (efficient), complete off-grid power

SPECIFICATIONS:
    Output: 100-500W at 24V or 48V DC
    Size: 400-600mm diameter
    Weight: 10-30 kg
    Build time: 15-30 hours
    
NOTE: This tier requires more precision, better materials,
      and stronger drive systems. Not a beginner project.

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CONFIGURATION OPTIONS:
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

OPTION A: Single Large Rotor

    Diameter: 400-500mm
    Magnets: 24-32 × 40mm+ neodymium
    Coils: 18-24 × AWG 14-16 wire
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                           │
    │                    SINGLE LARGE ROTOR                     │
    │                                                           │
    │           ╭─────────────────────────────────╮             │
    │         ╱ N   S   N   S   N   S   N   S   N  ╲           │
    │       ╱                                       ╲          │
    │      S                   ●                     S         │
    │     ╱                  SHAFT                    ╲        │
    │    N                                             N       │
    │     ╲                                           ╱        │
    │      S                                         S         │
    │       ╲                                       ╱          │
    │         ╲ N   S   N   S   N   S   N   S   N ╱            │
    │           ╰─────────────────────────────────╯             │
    │                                                           │
    │    24-32 magnets, large wire coils                       │
    │    High power but requires strong drive                  │
    │                                                           │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION B: Stacked Dual/Triple Rotor

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                           │
    │                    TRIPLE STACK                           │
    │                                                           │
    │   STATOR ─ ROTOR ─ STATOR ─ ROTOR ─ STATOR ─ ROTOR ─ STATOR
    │                                                           │
    │    ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══   │
    │    ║C║     ║M║     ║C║     ║M║     ║C║     ║M║     ║C║   │
    │    ║ ║     ║ ║     ║ ║     ║ ║     ║ ║     ║ ║     ║ ║   │
    │    ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══     ═══   │
    │      ↑       ↑       ↑       ↑       ↑       ↑       ↑    │
    │    Outer   Rotor  Middle   Rotor  Middle   Rotor   Outer │
    │    Stator    1    Stator     2    Stator     3    Stator │
    │                                                           │
    │    All rotors on same shaft, magnets aligned             │
    │    Middle stators have coils on both sides               │
    │    Triples power output for given diameter               │
    │                                                           │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION C: Parallel Multiple Units

    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                           │
    │                    PARALLEL UNITS                         │
    │                                                           │
    │       ┌─────────┐   ┌─────────┐   ┌─────────┐            │
    │       │  GEN 1  │   │  GEN 2  │   │  GEN 3  │            │
    │       │  TIER 2 │   │  TIER 2 │   │  TIER 2 │            │
    │       └────┬────┘   └────┬────┘   └────┬────┘            │
    │            │             │             │                  │
    │            ▼             ▼             ▼                  │
    │       ┌────────┐    ┌────────┐    ┌────────┐             │
    │       │ RECT 1 │    │ RECT 2 │    │ RECT 3 │             │
    │       └────┬───┘    └────┬───┘    └────┬───┘             │
    │            │             │             │                  │
    │            └──────┬──────┴──────┬──────┘                  │
    │                   │             │                         │
    │                   ▼             ▼                         │
    │              DC+ BUS       DC- BUS                       │
    │                   │             │                         │
    │                   └──────┬──────┘                         │
    │                          │                                │
    │                    ┌─────┴─────┐                          │
    │                    │  BATTERY  │                          │
    │                    │   BANK    │                          │
    │                    └───────────┘                          │
    │                                                           │
    │    Advantages:                                           │
    │    - Uses proven TIER 2 design                           │
    │    - Modular (add units as needed)                       │
    │    - Redundancy (one fails, others work)                 │
    │    - Easier to build and maintain                        │
    │                                                           │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
DETAILED SPECIFICATIONS (Option A - Single Large)
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

MAGNETS:
    Quantity: 24 (recommended) or 32
    Size: 40mm × 20mm × 10mm neodymium N42 grade (or better)
    Arrangement: Alternating N-S at 15° spacing (24 magnets)
    
    SOURCE: These quantities require:
    - 6-12 hard drives (2 magnets each)
    - OR scrap electric motors (cordless drills, e-bikes)
    - OR purchase (if available in region)
    
    MAGNET STRENGTH VERIFICATION:
    - Should lift 5+ kg steel
    - Should hold firmly through 10mm material

COILS:
    Quantity: 18 (3-phase, 6 per phase)
    Wire: AWG 14-16 (1.5-2mm diameter)
    Turns: 80-120 per coil
    Resistance: 0.5-1.5Ω per coil
    
    WIRE CALCULATION:
    Wire per coil = Turns × 4 × Coil_Width
    Example: 100 turns × 4 × 50mm = 20m per coil
    Total wire needed: 18 × 20m = 360m AWG 16
    
    WIRE SOURCES (360m needed):
    - 2-3 large transformer windings
    - 4-6 motor windings
    - Purchased copper wire

ROTOR:
    Diameter: 450mm
    Thickness: 20-25mm
    Material: Marine plywood, HDPE, or machined aluminum
    
    ALUMINUM CONSIDERATION:
    ⚠️ Aluminum causes eddy current losses
    - Energy lost as heat in rotor
    - Reduces efficiency 10-20%
    - May be acceptable trade-off for strength
    
    MITIGATION: Use slots between magnet positions
    
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                           │
    │        SLOTTED ALUMINUM ROTOR (reduces eddy currents)     │
    │                                                           │
    │              ╭───────────────────────╮                    │
    │            ╱ │ [M] │   │ [M] │   │ [M]│╲                 │
    │          ╱   └─────┘   └─────┘   └─────┘  ╲              │
    │        ╱        ↑         ↑         ↑       ╲            │
    │       │       Slots break eddy current paths │            │
    │        ╲                                    ╱             │
    │          ╲   ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐   ┌─────┐ ╱               │
    │            ╲ │ [M] │   │ [M] │   │ [M] │╱                 │
    │              ╰───────────────────────╯                    │
    │                                                           │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

STATORS:
    Diameter: 550mm OD × 350mm ID
    Thickness: 15-20mm
    Material: Plywood, HDPE, or fiberglass
    
    ⚠️ NO STEEL in stator for coreless design
    Steel adds cogging and complexity

BEARINGS:
    Type: Deep groove ball bearings
    Size: 6202 (15mm ID) or 6204 (20mm ID)
    Quantity: 2 (one each end)
    Quality: Sealed, grease-packed
    
    SOURCES:
    - Automotive (wheel bearings, alternator)
    - Industrial equipment
    - Agricultural machinery

SHAFT:
    Diameter: 15-20mm (match bearing ID)
    Length: 300mm
    Material: Steel rod, case hardened preferred
    
    SOURCES:
    - Machine shop stock
    - Automotive (axle shafts)
    - Cut-down tool shafts

FRAME:
    Type: Box frame (preferred) or heavy H-frame
    Material: Steel angle, heavy timber, or aluminum extrusion
    Size: 600mm × 600mm × 150mm
    
    REQUIREMENTS:
    - Rigid (no flexing under load)
    - Vibration resistant
    - Weather resistant (if outdoor)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
ELECTRICAL SYSTEM (TIER 3)
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

RECTIFIER:
    Type: 3-phase bridge (6 diodes)
    Diodes: 10A or higher rating
    Voltage: 100V minimum reverse voltage
    
    RECOMMENDED: Scavenge from welders, motor drives, or
                 purchase automotive bridge rectifier

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │              10A+ THREE-PHASE BRIDGE                       │
    │                                                            │
    │              ┌──[D1]──┐                                    │
    │    Phase A ──┤        ├──┐                                 │
    │              └──[D4]──┘  │                                 │
    │                          │                                 │
    │              ┌──[D2]──┐  │                                 │
    │    Phase B ──┤        ├──┼── DC+ ──→                       │
    │              └──[D5]──┘  │                                 │
    │                          │                                 │
    │              ┌──[D3]──┐  │                                 │
    │    Phase C ──┤        ├──┘                                 │
    │              └──[D6]──┘                                    │
    │                   │                                        │
    │                   └───────── DC- ──→                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    HEATSINK REQUIRED for sustained 100W+ operation         │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

FILTER CAPACITOR BANK:
    Capacitance: 10,000-50,000 µF
    Voltage: 63V or higher (for 48V system)
    
    CONSTRUCTION:
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    DC+ ──┬────┬────┬────┬────┬──→ To Controller           │
    │          │    │    │    │    │                             │
    │        ──┴── ──┴── ──┴── ──┴── ──┴──                       │
    │        4700  4700  4700  4700  4700  µF                    │
    │        ──┬── ──┬── ──┬── ──┬── ──┬──                       │
    │          │    │    │    │    │                             │
    │    DC- ──┴────┴────┴────┴────┴──→ To Controller           │
    │                                                            │
    │    5× 4700µF in parallel = 23,500µF total                 │
    │    SOURCE: Computer power supplies (each has 1-2 large)   │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CHARGE CONTROLLER:
    Type: MPPT (Maximum Power Point Tracking) preferred
          PWM (Pulse Width Modulation) acceptable
    
    Rating: Match expected power output
            100W system → 10A+ controller
            300W system → 25A+ controller
            500W system → 40A+ controller
    
    SOURCES:
    - Solar charge controllers (most common)
    - Wind turbine controllers
    - Electric vehicle controllers
    
    ⚠️ VOLTAGE MATCHING:
    Generator voltage must exceed battery voltage
    For 48V battery: Need 60V+ from generator
    Check controller input voltage range

BATTERY BANK:
    Type: Deep cycle lead-acid, AGM, or lithium
    Capacity: Size for your load + storage days
    
    EXAMPLE CALCULATION:
    
    │ Load Description      │ Watts │ Hours/Day │ Wh/Day │
    │───────────────────────│───────│───────────│────────│
    │ LED lighting          │   30  │     6     │   180  │
    │ Phone charging        │   10  │     4     │    40  │
    │ Laptop                │   50  │     4     │   200  │
    │ Small refrigerator    │   50  │    12     │   600  │
    │ WiFi router           │   10  │    24     │   240  │
    │───────────────────────│───────│───────────│────────│
    │ TOTAL                 │  150  │           │  1260  │
    
    Daily consumption: 1260 Wh
    
    For 2 days autonomy (no generation):
    Battery capacity needed = 1260 × 2 = 2520 Wh
    
    At 24V: 2520 Wh / 24V = 105 Ah battery bank
    At 48V: 2520 Wh / 48V = 52.5 Ah battery bank
    
    Add 50% for lead-acid (don't discharge below 50%):
    24V system: 210 Ah (4× 6V 200Ah golf cart batteries)
    48V system: 105 Ah (4× 12V 100Ah batteries in series)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
DRIVE SYSTEMS (TIER 3)
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Human power alone insufficient for sustained 100W+ output.
Recommended drive sources:

OPTION A: WIND TURBINE

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │                    HORIZONTAL AXIS WIND TURBINE            │
    │                                                            │
    │                         HUB                                │
    │                          │                                 │
    │            BLADE ────────┼──────── BLADE                   │
    │                          │                                 │
    │                          │                                 │
    │                       BLADE                                │
    │                          │                                 │
    │                    ┌─────┴─────┐                           │
    │                    │ GENERATOR │                           │
    │                    └─────┬─────┘                           │
    │                          │                                 │
    │                    ┌─────┴─────┐                           │
    │                    │   TOWER   │                           │
    │                    │           │                           │
    │                    │           │                           │
    │                    └───────────┘                           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    
    BLADE SPECIFICATIONS:
    - 3 blades (standard, balanced)
    - Diameter: 2-3m for 100-300W
    - Material: Wood, PVC pipe, fiberglass
    
    POWER OUTPUT (approximate):
    
    │ Wind Speed │ 2m Rotor │ 3m Rotor │
    │  (m/s)     │  Power   │  Power   │
    │────────────│──────────│──────────│
    │     3      │    5W    │   15W    │
    │     5      │   25W    │   60W    │
    │     7      │   70W    │  160W    │
    │    10      │  200W    │  450W    │
    │    12      │  350W    │  780W    │
    
    BLADE DESIGN (Simple Carved Wooden):
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ╭────────────────────────────────────────────╮          │
    │    │                                            │          │
    │    │        BLADE CROSS SECTION (TIP)           │← Thin    │
    │    │                                            │          │
    │    ╰────────────────────────────────────────────╯          │
    │                                                            │
    │    ╭────────────────────────────────────╮                  │
    │    │                                    │                  │
    │    │     BLADE CROSS SECTION (ROOT)     │← Thick          │
    │    │                                    │                  │
    │    ╰────────────────────────────────────╯                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    Material: 50×200mm softwood plank                       │
    │    Taper from root (thick) to tip (thin)                   │
    │    Carve curved leading edge, flat trailing edge           │
    │    Sand smooth, seal with paint/varnish                    │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION B: WATER TURBINE

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │                    PELTON WHEEL (High Head)                │
    │                                                            │
    │    For streams with significant drop (5m+ head)            │
    │                                                            │
    │            WATER SOURCE (UPHILL)                           │
    │                   │                                        │
    │                   │ PIPE (50-100mm)                        │
    │                   │                                        │
    │                   ▼                                        │
    │               [NOZZLE]                                     │
    │                  → → → → →                                 │
    │                     ╭───╮                                  │
    │                    ╱│ ● │╲   ← Pelton wheel                │
    │                   ╱ │   │ ╲    with cups                   │
    │                     │   │                                  │
    │                     │ G │ ← Generator                      │
    │                     │ E │                                  │
    │                     │ N │                                  │
    │                     └───┘                                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    POWER = Head(m) × Flow(L/s) × 7 (approximate watts)     │
    │    Example: 10m head × 5 L/s × 7 = 350W potential          │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │                    UNDERSHOT WHEEL (Low Head)              │
    │                                                            │
    │    For rivers/streams with low drop (< 2m head)            │
    │                                                            │
    │          WATER FLOW                                        │
    │     ════════════════════════                               │
    │     → → → → → → → → → → → →                               │
    │          ╭─────────────╮                                   │
    │         ╱│ ╭─╮   ╭─╮   │╲                                  │
    │        ╱ │ │ │   │ │   │ ╲                                 │
    │     ══╱══│═│═│═══│═│═══│══╲══ WATER LEVEL                  │
    │           │ │     │ │                                      │
    │           │ ╰─╮ ╭─╯ │      ← Paddle wheel                  │
    │           │   │●│   │        (blades in water)             │
    │           │ ╭─╯ ╰─╮ │                                      │
    │           └─┴─────┴─┘                                      │
    │                │                                           │
    │           ┌────┴────┐                                      │
    │           │GENERATOR│                                      │
    │           └─────────┘                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    Lower efficiency than Pelton but works with any flow    │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION C: PEDAL GENERATOR (Bicycle Frame)

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │                    BICYCLE FRAME GENERATOR                 │
    │                                                            │
    │         SEAT                                               │
    │           ○                                                │
    │            ╲                                               │
    │             ╲                                              │
    │              ╲                                             │
    │    HANDLEBARS ╲                                            │

    │                │   FRAME                                   │
    │                │                                           │

    │   ╱   ╲                 ╱   ╲                              │

TIER 4: FULL SCALE (500W+) - REFERENCE

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    TIER 4: FULL SCALE (REFERENCE ONLY)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PURPOSE: Complete household power, small workshop, agricultural
         pumping, community micro-grid

SPECIFICATIONS:
    Output: 500W - 5kW+
    Size: 500mm - 1000mm+ diameter
    Weight: 30-200+ kg
    
NOTE: This tier exceeds DIY scavenging scope.
      Requires purchased components, precision tools,
      or commercial generator conversion.

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
APPROACHES:
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

OPTION A: Automotive Alternator Conversion

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CAR ALTERNATOR → PERMANENT MAGNET CONVERSION            │
    │                                                            │
    │    Standard alternator uses electromagnet rotor            │
    │    Requires battery power to generate                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    CONVERSION:                                             │
    │    1. Remove electromagnetic rotor                         │
    │    2. Machine or 3D print new rotor hub                    │
    │    3. Mount strong neodymium magnets on rotor              │
    │    4. Reassemble                                           │
    │                                                            │
    │    RESULT:                                                 │
    │    - Self-exciting (no battery needed to start)            │
    │    - Low-RPM capable (300-500 RPM vs 2000+ stock)          │
    │    - 500W-1500W output depending on alternator size        │
    │                                                            │
    │    SOURCES:                                                │
    │    - Any car, truck, tractor alternator                    │
    │    - Large alternators from trucks/buses = more power      │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION B: Motor Conversion (Treadmill/Industrial)

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    PERMANENT MAGNET DC MOTOR → GENERATOR                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    Many DC motors with permanent magnets work as           │
    │    generators when spun mechanically.                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    BEST SOURCES:                                           │
    │    - Treadmill motors (90-180V DC, 2-3 HP)                │
    │    - Electric scooter/bike motors                          │
    │    - Golf cart motors                                      │
    │    - Industrial servo motors                               │
    │                                                            │
    │    TESTING:                                                │
    │    1. Connect multimeter to motor terminals                │
    │    2. Spin shaft by hand                                   │
    │    3. If voltage generated → usable as generator           │
    │                                                            │
    │    OUTPUT:                                                 │
    │    - Treadmill motor at 500 RPM → 500-1500W              │
    │    - Already built for continuous duty                     │
    │    - Already has bearings, shaft, mounting                 │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPTION C: Scaled Multiple TIER 2/3 Units

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    PARALLEL GENERATOR FARM                                 │
    │                                                            │
    │    ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐  ┌─────┐            │
    │    │GEN1 │  │GEN2 │  │GEN3 │  │GEN4 │  │GEN5 │            │
    │    │100W │  │100W │  │100W │  │100W │  │100W │            │
    │    └──┬──┘  └──┬──┘  └──┬──┘  └──┬──┘  └──┬──┘            │
    │       │        │        │        │        │                │
    │       ▼        ▼        ▼        ▼        ▼                │
    │    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐            │
    │    │           COMMON DC BUS                  │            │
    │    │              48V DC                      │            │
    │    └──────────────────┬───────────────────────┘            │
    │                       │                                    │
    │                       ▼                                    │
    │              ┌────────────────┐                            │
    │              │ BATTERY BANK   │                            │
    │              │    500Ah+      │                            │
    │              └────────────────┘                            │
    │                       │                                    │
    │                       ▼                                    │
    │              ┌────────────────┐                            │
    │              │   INVERTER     │ → 120V/240V AC             │
    │              │   1000W+       │                            │
    │              └────────────────┘                            │
    │                                                            │
    │    ADVANTAGES:                                             │
    │    - Redundancy (one fails, 80% capacity remains)          │
    │    - Modular growth (add units as needed)                  │
    │    - Uses proven smaller designs                           │
    │    - Easier maintenance                                    │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PART IV: APPLICATIONS & USE CASES
CHAPTER 10: PRACTICAL APPLICATIONS

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    PRACTICAL APPLICATION GUIDE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

10.1 DEVICE POWER REQUIREMENTS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    │ Device                  │ Watts    │ Generator Tier │
    │─────────────────────────│──────────│────────────────│
    │ LED bulb (single)       │   3-10   │     TIER 0     │
    │ Phone charging          │   5-15   │     TIER 0     │
    │ Small radio             │   2-10   │     TIER 0     │
    │ LED strip (1m)          │   5-15   │     TIER 0     │
    │ USB fan                 │   2-5    │     TIER 0     │
    │─────────────────────────│──────────│────────────────│
    │ Tablet charging         │  10-20   │     TIER 1     │
    │ Multiple LED bulbs      │  15-30   │     TIER 1     │
    │ Small speaker           │   5-20   │     TIER 1     │
    │ Emergency radio         │   5-15   │     TIER 1     │
    │─────────────────────────│──────────│────────────────│
    │ Laptop                  │  30-60   │     TIER 2     │
    │ LED TV (small)          │  30-50   │     TIER 2     │
    │ WiFi router             │  10-20   │     TIER 2     │
    │ 12V water pump          │  30-100  │     TIER 2     │
    │ DC refrigerator (small) │  40-80   │     TIER 2     │
    │─────────────────────────│──────────│────────────────│
    │ Desktop computer        │ 100-300  │     TIER 3     │
    │ Power tools (light)     │ 100-500  │     TIER 3     │
    │ AC refrigerator         │ 100-400  │     TIER 3     │
    │ Washing machine         │ 200-500  │     TIER 3     │
    │ Multiple room lighting  │ 50-200   │     TIER 3     │
    │─────────────────────────│──────────│────────────────│
    │ Air conditioning        │ 500-2000 │     TIER 4     │
    │ Electric heating        │ 500-3000 │     TIER 4     │
    │ Heavy power tools       │ 500-2000 │     TIER 4     │
    │ Electric cooking        │1000-3000 │     TIER 4     │

10.2 CONNECTION METHODS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

DIRECT DC CONNECTION (Simplest):

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    GENERATOR → RECTIFIER → REGULATOR → DC DEVICE           │
    │                                                            │
    │    Suitable for:                                           │
    │    - 5V devices (USB)                                      │
    │    - 12V devices (car accessories, LED strips)             │
    │    - 24V devices (some tools, solar equipment)             │
    │                                                            │
    │    VOLTAGE MATCHING:                                       │
    │    Device voltage must match regulator output              │
    │                                                            │
    │    USB PORT ADDITION:                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    12V DC ──[Buck Converter]──→ 5V ──[USB Socket]          │
    │                  │                       │                 │
    │           (LM2596 module                 │                 │
    │            or car USB charger)           │                 │
    │                                     ┌────┴────┐            │
    │                                     │  PHONE  │            │
    │                                     └─────────┘            │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

BATTERY BUFFERED SYSTEM (Recommended):

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    GENERATOR → CHARGE CONTROLLER → BATTERY → DEVICES       │
    │                                                            │
    │    ADVANTAGES:                                             │
    │    - Stable voltage regardless of generator speed          │
    │    - Power available when not generating                   │
    │    - Can handle surge loads (motor starting)               │
    │    - Protects sensitive electronics                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    BASIC SETUP:                                            │
    │                                                            │
    │    ┌─────────┐     ┌───────────┐     ┌─────────┐          │
    │    │   GEN   │────→│  CHARGE   │────→│ BATTERY │          │
    │    │  (DC)   │     │CONTROLLER │     │  12V    │          │
    │    └─────────┘     └───────────┘     └────┬────┘          │
    │                                           │                │
    │         ┌─────────────────────────────────┤                │
    │         │              │                  │                │
    │         ▼              ▼                  ▼                │
    │    ┌─────────┐   ┌──────────┐      ┌──────────┐           │
    │    │  FUSE   │   │  12V DC  │      │ INVERTER │           │
    │    │  BOX    │   │ OUTLETS  │      │ (if AC)  │           │
    │    └─────────┘   └──────────┘      └──────────┘           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

INVERTER FOR AC DEVICES:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FOR DEVICES REQUIRING AC POWER (120V/240V):             │
    │                                                            │
    │    BATTERY (12V/24V/48V) ──→ INVERTER ──→ AC DEVICES       │
    │                                                            │
    │    INVERTER TYPES:                                         │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ Type          │ Cost   │ Compatibility │ Efficiency │ │
    │    │───────────────│────────│───────────────│────────────│ │
    │    │ Modified Sine │ Low    │ Most devices  │ 80-90%     │ │
    │    │ Pure Sine     │ High   │ All devices   │ 85-95%     │ │
    │                                                            │
    │    ⚠️ MODIFIED SINE WAVE may cause:                        │
    │    - Buzzing in audio equipment                            │
    │    - Overheating in some motors                            │
    │    - Malfunction of sensitive electronics                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    PURE SINE WAVE required for:                            │
    │    - Medical equipment                                     │
    │    - Laser printers                                        │
    │    - Some refrigerators                                    │
    │    - Audio/video equipment                                 │
    │    - Motor-driven tools                                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    INVERTER SIZING:                                        │
    │    Peak watts = 2-3× running watts (for motor starting)    │
    │    Example: 100W refrigerator → 300W inverter minimum      │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

10.3 SPECIFIC USE CASES
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

USE CASE A: EMERGENCY PHONE CHARGING

    SCENARIO: Power outage, need to charge phones
    
    SOLUTION: TIER 0 hand-crank generator
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ┌──────────┐    ┌────────┐    ┌─────────┐              │
    │    │  TIER 0  │───→│  5V    │───→│  USB    │──→ PHONE     │
    │    │ HAND GEN │    │  REG   │    │ SOCKET  │              │
    │    └──────────┘    └────────┘    └─────────┘              │
    │         ↑                                                  │
    │      [CRANK]                                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    OPERATION:                                              │
    │    - Crank at 2-3 revolutions/second                      │
    │    - 10-15 minutes cranking = ~5-10% phone charge         │
    │    - 1-2 hours = full charge                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    TIP: Charge a power bank instead of phone directly     │
    │         - Power bank tolerates variable input better      │
    │         - Then phone charges from power bank normally     │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

USE CASE B: OFF-GRID CABIN LIGHTING

    SCENARIO: Remote cabin with no grid power
    
    SOLUTION: TIER 1/2 generator + battery + LED lights
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    WIND/WATER/PEDAL                                        │
    │          │                                                 │
    │          ▼                                                 │
    │    ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────┐    ┌─────────┐           │
    │    │  TIER 2  │───→│  CHARGE   │───→│  12V    │           │
    │    │   GEN    │    │ CONTROLLER│    │ BATTERY │           │
    │    └──────────┘    └───────────┘    └────┬────┘           │
    │                                          │                 │
    │    ┌─────────────────────────────────────┤                 │
    │    │              │              │       │                 │
    │    ▼              ▼              ▼       ▼                 │
    │  ┌────┐        ┌────┐        ┌────┐  ┌──────┐             │
    │  │LED │        │LED │        │LED │  │ USB  │             │
    │  │ 5W │        │ 5W │        │ 5W │  │PORTS │             │
    │  └────┘        └────┘        └────┘  └──────┘             │
    │  Kitchen       Living        Bedroom   Charging           │
    │                                                            │
    │    DAILY ENERGY BUDGET:                                    │
    │    3× LED lights × 5W × 5 hours = 75 Wh/day              │
    │    Phone charging = 20 Wh/day                             │
    │    Radio = 10 Wh/day                                      │
    │    TOTAL: ~105 Wh/day                                     │
    │                                                            │
    │    GENERATION REQUIREMENT:                                 │
    │    At 50W average output: 105 Wh / 50W = 2.1 hours/day   │
    │                                                            │
    │    BATTERY REQUIREMENT (2 day autonomy):                   │
    │    105 Wh × 2 days × 2 (50% DoD) / 12V = 35 Ah           │
    │    → Use 50-100 Ah 12V battery                            │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

USE CASE C: FIELD COMMUNICATION STATION

    SCENARIO: Remote research station, emergency communication
    
    SOLUTION: TIER 2 generator for radio/satellite equipment
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────┐    ┌─────────┐           │
    │    │  WIND    │───→│  CHARGE   │───→│  24V    │           │
    │    │GENERATOR │    │ CONTROLLER│    │ BATTERY │           │
    │    │  TIER 2  │    └───────────┘    └────┬────┘           │
    │    └──────────┘                          │                 │
    │         +                                │                 │
    │    ┌──────────┐                          │                 │
    │    │  PEDAL   │──────────────────────────┤                 │
    │    │ BACKUP   │                          │                 │
    │    └──────────┘                          │                 │
    │                    ┌─────────────────────┤                 │
    │                    │           │         │                 │
    │                    ▼           ▼         ▼                 │
    │              ┌──────────┐ ┌────────┐ ┌────────┐           │
    │              │HF RADIO  │ │SATELLITE│ │ LAPTOP │           │
    │              │   50W    │ │  PHONE  │ │   50W  │           │
    │              └──────────┘ └────────┘ └────────┘           │
    │                                                            │
    │    REDUNDANCY: Wind + Pedal ensures power availability    │
    │    PRIORITY LOADS: Radio first, satellite, then laptop   │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

USE CASE D: SMALL WATER PUMPING

    SCENARIO: Garden irrigation from well/stream
    
    SOLUTION: TIER 2/3 generator driving 12V or 24V pump
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    WATER SOURCE (stream, dam)                              │
    │          │                                                 │
    │          ▼                                                 │
    │    ┌────────────┐         ┌──────────────┐                │
    │    │   WATER    │         │  GENERATOR   │                │
    │    │   WHEEL    │ SHAFT   │   TIER 3     │                │
    │    │  or TURBINE│════════>│  Direct Drive│                │
    │    └────────────┘         └──────┬───────┘                │
    │                                  │                         │
    │                                  ▼                         │
    │                          ┌─────────────┐                   │
    │                          │   BATTERY   │                   │
    │                          │    24V      │                   │
    │                          └──────┬──────┘                   │
    │                                 │                          │
    │                                 ▼                          │
    │                          ┌─────────────┐                   │
    │                          │  24V PUMP   │                   │
    │                          │  (50-100W)  │                   │
    │                          └──────┬──────┘                   │
    │                                 │                          │
    │                                 ▼                          │
    │    ════════════════════════════════════════════            │
    │              IRRIGATION PIPES/SPRINKLERS                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    ELEGANCE: Water powers generator that powers pump       │
    │              Self-sustaining system                        │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

USE CASE E: COMMUNITY CHARGING STATION

    SCENARIO: Village with no grid power
    
    SOLUTION: TIER 3 generator + battery bank + multiple outlets
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │         WIND TURBINE            PEDAL STATIONS             │
    │              │                    │     │                  │
    │              │        ┌───────────┘     │                  │
    │              ▼        ▼                 ▼                  │

CHAPTER 11: MAINTENANCE & LONGEVITY

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    MAINTENANCE GUIDE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

11.1 ROUTINE MAINTENANCE SCHEDULE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    │ Interval  │ Task                                  │ Tier │
    │───────────│───────────────────────────────────────│──────│
    │ DAILY     │ Visual check (unusual sounds/motion)  │ All  │
    │           │ Check output voltage (if metered)     │ All  │
    │───────────│───────────────────────────────────────│──────│
    │ WEEKLY    │ Clean debris from air gap             │ All  │
    │           │ Check wire connections (secure?)      │ All  │
    │           │ Inspect magnets (still attached?)     │ All  │
    │           │ Check bearing smooth rotation         │ All  │
    │───────────│───────────────────────────────────────│──────│
    │ MONTHLY   │ Clean all surfaces                    │ All  │
    │           │ Tighten all fasteners                 │ All  │
    │           │ Measure coil resistance (record)      │ All  │
    │           │ Check air gap dimension               │ 2+   │
    │           │ Lubricate non-sealed bearings         │ All  │
    │           │ Inspect wire insulation               │ All  │
    │───────────│───────────────────────────────────────│──────│
    │ QUARTERLY │ Full performance test (record)        │ All  │
    │           │ Check all electrical connections      │ All  │
    │           │ Inspect frame for cracks/damage       │ 2+   │
    │           │ Test battery condition (if used)      │ 2+   │
    │───────────│───────────────────────────────────────│──────│
    │ ANNUALLY  │ Replace worn bearings                 │ All  │
    │           │ Recoat/reseal wooden components       │ All  │
    │           │ Replace worn wires/connections        │ All  │
    │           │ Full disassembly inspection           │ 3+   │

11.2 BEARING MAINTENANCE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

SEALED BALL BEARINGS:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    MAINTENANCE: Minimal (lifetime sealed)                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    REPLACEMENT INDICATORS:                                 │
    │    - Grinding/clicking sounds                              │
    │    - Visible play (wobble when shaft pushed)               │
    │    - Increased resistance to rotation                      │
    │    - Visible rust or damage                                │
    │                                                            │
    │    REPLACEMENT PROCEDURE:                                  │
    │    1. Remove shaft from frame                              │
    │    2. Support frame plate, press/tap bearing out           │
    │    3. Clean bearing seat                                   │
    │    4. Press/tap new bearing in (support outer race only)   │
    │    5. Reassemble                                           │
    │                                                            │
    │    LIFESPAN:                                               │
    │    - Light duty: 10,000+ hours                            │
    │    - Heavy duty: 3,000-5,000 hours                        │
    │    - Replace every 1-3 years for continuous use           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

OPEN BALL BEARINGS (Require lubrication):

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    LUBRICATION PROCEDURE:                                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    1. Clean exterior of bearing                            │
    │    2. Apply light machine oil or bearing grease            │
    │    3. Rotate shaft to distribute lubricant                 │
    │    4. Wipe excess                                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    LUBRICANT OPTIONS:                                      │
    │    - Light machine oil (sewing machine oil)                │
    │    - Lithium grease (longer lasting)                       │
    │    - Bicycle chain oil                                     │
    │    - Motor oil (in emergency)                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    FREQUENCY:                                              │
    │    - Monthly for continuous use                            │
    │    - Quarterly for intermittent use                        │
    │    - Immediately if squeaking                              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

DIY BEARINGS (Bottle cap, wood):

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    MAINTENANCE: Frequent lubrication required              │
    │                                                            │
    │    - Lubricate every 10-50 hours of operation             │
    │    - Use any oil, grease, soap, wax, or fat               │
    │    - Inspect for wear (elongated hole)                    │
    │    - Replace when excessive play develops                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    WEAR REDUCTION:                                         │
    │    - Keep well lubricated                                  │
    │    - Avoid overloading                                     │
    │    - Use harder materials (hardwood > softwood)            │
    │    - Consider upgrading to ball bearings                   │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

11.3 MAGNET MAINTENANCE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    NEODYMIUM MAGNETS:                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    FAILURE MODES:                                          │
    │    1. Corrosion (coating fails, magnet rusts)              │
    │    2. Thermal demagnetization (above 80°C)                 │
    │    3. Mechanical damage (cracks, chips)                    │
    │    4. Detachment from rotor                                │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Coat exposed magnets (paint, epoxy, tape)             │
    │    - Ensure adequate cooling (don't seal completely)       │
    │    - Secure mechanically (not just adhesive)               │
    │    - Handle carefully (they're brittle)                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    REPLACEMENT:                                            │
    │    If magnet weakens significantly:                        │
    │    1. Remove old magnet (heat if glued)                   │
    │    2. Clean surface                                        │
    │    3. Install new magnet (same size, same orientation)     │
    │    4. Secure with epoxy + mechanical retention             │
    │                                                            │
    │    TESTING MAGNET STRENGTH:                                │
    │    - Compare to new magnet (should be similar)             │
    │    - Count paper clips it holds (record baseline)          │
    │    - Measure voltage output (compare to original)          │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FERRITE (CERAMIC) MAGNETS:                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    MORE DURABLE than neodymium:                            │
    │    - No corrosion                                          │
    │    - Higher temperature tolerance (up to 300°C)            │
    │    - But weaker (need more/larger magnets)                 │
    │                                                            │
    │    FAILURE MODES:                                          │
    │    1. Cracking/chipping (brittle)                          │
    │    2. Detachment                                           │
    │                                                            │
    │    These magnets essentially last forever if not           │
    │    physically damaged.                                     │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

11.4 COIL MAINTENANCE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FAILURE MODES:                                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    1. OPEN CIRCUIT (broken wire)                           │
    │       Symptom: No output, infinite resistance              │
    │       Cause: Vibration fatigue, corrosion, damage          │
    │       Fix: Find break, solder repair or rewind             │
    │                                                            │
    │    2. SHORT CIRCUIT (insulation failure)                   │
    │       Symptom: Low output, low resistance, heating         │
    │       Cause: Insulation wear, moisture, overheating        │
    │       Fix: Rewind coil with new wire                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    3. HIGH RESISTANCE (corrosion, loose connections)       │
    │       Symptom: Low power output, heating at connections    │
    │       Cause: Corrosion, poor solder joints, wire damage    │
    │       Fix: Clean connections, resolder, replace wire       │
    │                                                            │
    │    MONITORING:                                             │
    │    - Record coil resistance at installation                │
    │    - Re-measure monthly/quarterly                          │
    │    - Resistance should remain stable (±10%)                │
    │    - Significant change = problem developing               │
    │                                                            │
    │    PROTECTION:                                             │
    │    - Pot coils in epoxy (moisture protection)              │
    │    - Tape wrap (mechanical protection)                     │
    │    - Ensure leads are strain-relieved                      │
    │    - Avoid overloading (overheating)                       │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

11.5 ELECTRICAL SYSTEM MAINTENANCE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CONNECTIONS:                                            │
    │                                                            │
    │    CHECK ALL CONNECTIONS FOR:                              │
    │    - Tightness (loose = resistance = heat)                 │
    │    - Corrosion (green/white deposits)                      │
    │    - Discoloration (evidence of overheating)               │
    │                                                            │
    │    MAINTENANCE:                                            │
    │    - Clean corroded terminals (sandpaper, vinegar)         │
    │    - Apply anti-corrosion grease (petroleum jelly works)   │
    │    - Re-solder questionable joints                         │
    │    - Replace damaged connectors                            │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    RECTIFIER DIODES:                                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    FAILURE MODES:                                          │
    │    - Open (no output on half cycle)                        │
    │    - Shorted (no rectification, DC voltage drops)          │
    │                                                            │
    │    TESTING:                                                │
    │    Multimeter diode test mode:                             │
    │    - Forward: Should show 0.5-0.7V                         │
    │    - Reverse: Should show OL (open)                        │
    │    - If shorted in both directions: Replace                │
    │    - If open in both directions: Replace                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    REPLACEMENT:                                            │
    │    - Match or exceed original rating                       │
    │    - Observe polarity (band marks cathode)                 │
    │    - Use heatsink if in high-power application             │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CAPACITORS:                                             │
    │                                                            │
    │    FAILURE SIGNS:                                          │
    │    - Bulging top (electrolytic)                            │
    │    - Leaking electrolyte                                   │
    │    - Visible damage/burn marks                             │
    │    - Reduced filtering (more ripple)                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    TESTING:                                                │
    │    1. Discharge capacitor (short with resistor)            │
    │    2. Measure capacitance (if meter available)             │
    │    3. Compare to rated value                               │
    │    4. Should be within -20% to +50% of rated               │
    │                                                            │
    │    REPLACEMENT:                                            │
    │    - Match or exceed voltage rating                        │
    │    - Match or exceed capacitance                           │
    │    - Observe polarity (electrolytic)                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    LIFESPAN:                                               │
    │    - Electrolytic: 5-15 years typical                      │
    │    - Film/ceramic: 20+ years                               │
    │    - High temperature accelerates aging                    │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

11.6 BATTERY MAINTENANCE (If used)
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    LEAD-ACID BATTERIES:                                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    FLOODED (Wet Cell):                                     │
    │    - Check water level monthly                             │
    │    - Add distilled water only (not tap)                    │
    │    - Fill to line (not over)                               │
    │    - Clean terminals quarterly                             │
    │    - Equalize charge quarterly (if controller supports)    │
    │                                                            │
    │    SEALED (AGM/Gel):                                       │
    │    - Clean terminals quarterly                             │
    │    - Check for swelling (overcharge damage)                │
    │    - No water addition needed                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    ALL LEAD-ACID:                                          │
    │    - Don't discharge below 50% (reduces life)              │
    │    - Store fully charged                                   │
    │    - Recharge if sitting unused > 1 month                  │
    │    - Keep clean and dry                                    │
    │    - Ensure good ventilation (hydrogen gas)                │
    │                                                            │
    │    TESTING:                                                │
    │    - Voltage test: 12.6V+ = full, <12.0V = needs charge   │
    │    - Load test: Voltage should stay above 10.5V @ load     │
    │    - Specific gravity (flooded): 1.265 = full              │
    │                                                            │
    │    LIFESPAN:                                               │
    │    - Flooded: 3-5 years with good care                     │
    │    - AGM: 5-8 years with good care                         │
    │    - Gel: 5-10 years with good care                        │
    │    - Lithium: 10-15 years                                  │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

11.7 MAINTENANCE RECORD TEMPLATE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    GENERATOR MAINTENANCE LOG                               │
    │                                                            │
    │    Unit ID: _____________  Build Date: ___________         │

    │    - Load voltage @ 200 RPM, 1A: _______ V                │
    │    - Magnet test (paper clips): _______                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────   │
    │    │ Date   │ Hours │ Task Performed    │ Findings      │  │
    │    │────────│───────│───────────────────│───────────────│  │
    │    │        │       │                   │               │  │
    │    │        │       │                   │               │  │
    │    │        │       │                   │               │  │
    │    │        │       │                   │               │  │
