CHAPTER 12: SAFETY

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    COMPREHENSIVE SAFETY GUIDE
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

12.1 MAGNET SAFETY
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ⚠️⚠️⚠️ NEODYMIUM MAGNETS ARE DANGEROUS ⚠️⚠️⚠️

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CRUSHING HAZARD:                                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    Strong magnets can:                                     │
    │    - Accelerate toward each other at high speed            │
    │    - Trap fingers between magnet and steel                 │
    │    - Cause blood blisters, broken bones, amputations       │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Always handle with thick gloves                       │
    │    - Keep magnets separated with cardboard/wood spacers    │
    │    - Never let magnets snap together uncontrolled          │
    │    - Approach steel objects slowly                         │
    │    - Work over soft surface (drop won't shatter)           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    SHATTERING HAZARD:                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    Neodymium magnets are brittle and can:                  │
    │    - Shatter on effect (dropping, collision)               │
    │    - Send sharp fragments flying                           │
    │    - Cause eye injuries                                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Wear eye protection when handling                     │
    │    - Wrap magnets in tape for protection                   │
    │    - Work over contained area                              │
    │    - Keep face away from magnets                           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    SWALLOWING HAZARD:                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    ⚠️ LETHAL IF MULTIPLE MAGNETS SWALLOWED ⚠️               │
    │                                                            │
    │    Magnets can:                                            │
    │    - Attract through intestinal walls                      │
    │    - Pinch and perforate intestines                        │
    │    - Cause internal bleeding, sepsis, death                │
    │                                                            │
    │    THIS IS A MEDICAL EMERGENCY                             │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - KEEP ALL MAGNETS AWAY FROM CHILDREN                   │
    │    - KEEP ALL MAGNETS AWAY FROM PETS                       │
    │    - Store magnets in locked/secure container              │
    │    - Account for all magnets                               │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ELECTRONIC INTERFERENCE:                                │
    │                                                            │
    │    Strong magnets can damage or erase:                     │
    │    - Credit cards, ID cards, hotel keys                    │
    │    - Hard drives, magnetic tapes                           │
    │    - CRT monitors                                          │
    │    - Pacemakers (LIFE THREATENING)                         │
    │    - Insulin pumps                                         │
    │    - Cochlear implants                                     │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Keep magnets 1m+ from electronics                     │
    │    - Remove cards/electronics before handling magnets      │
    │    - Warn anyone with medical implants                     │
    │    - Keep generators away from medical facilities          │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

12.2 ELECTRICAL SAFETY
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    VOLTAGE HAZARDS:                                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ Voltage      │ Risk Level      │                      │
    │    │──────────────│─────────────────│                      │
    │    │ <12V DC      │ Generally safe  │ (can still burn)    │
    │    │ 12-48V DC    │ Low risk        │ (shock possible)    │
    │    │ >48V DC      │ Hazardous       │ (lethal possible)   │
    │    │ Any AC       │ Higher risk     │ (same voltage more  │
    │    │              │                 │  dangerous than DC)  │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Insulate all live connections                         │
    │    - Use appropriate wire gauge                            │
    │    - Install fuses/breakers                                │
    │    - Don't work on live circuits                           │
    │    - Use one hand when testing (reduces heart path)        │
    │    - Keep dry                                              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    SHORT CIRCUIT HAZARDS:                                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    Short circuits cause:                                   │
    │    - Fire (wires overheat, insulation burns)               │
    │    - Explosions (batteries can explode)                    │
    │    - Burns (hot wires, sparks)                             │
    │    - Component damage                                      │
    │                                                            │
      │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Always use fuses appropriately sized                  │
    │    - Double-check wiring before energizing                 │
    │    - Use insulated tools                                   │
    │    - Keep metal objects away from terminals                │
    │    - Secure all connections (loose = arcing)               │
    │    - Separate positive and negative wire runs              │
    │    - Inspect insulation regularly                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    FUSE SIZING:                                            │
    │    Fuse rating = 125-150% of expected maximum current      │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ Expected Current │ Fuse Size │                        │
    │    │──────────────────│───────────│                        │
    │    │      1A          │   1.5A    │                        │
    │    │      5A          │   7.5A    │                        │
    │    │     10A          │  15A      │                        │
    │    │     20A          │  25-30A   │                        │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CAPACITOR HAZARDS:                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    Large capacitors store dangerous energy:                │
    │    - Can deliver lethal shock even when disconnected       │
    │    - Can remain charged for hours/days                     │
    │    - Higher voltage = more dangerous                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    ENERGY STORED:                                          │
    │    E = 0.5 × C × V²                                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    Example: 4700µF at 50V                                 │
    │    E = 0.5 × 0.0047 × 50² = 5.9 Joules                   │
    │    (Enough to cause serious burns/shock)                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    SAFE HANDLING:                                          │
    │    1. Always assume capacitor is charged                   │
    │    2. Discharge through resistor before handling           │
    │       (100-1000Ω, 1-5W for 10+ seconds)                   │
    │    3. Verify discharge with multimeter                     │
    │    4. Don't short directly (explosion risk)                │
    │    5. Observe polarity (reverse = explosion)               │
    │                                                            │
    │    DISCHARGE TOOL:                                         │
    │                                                            │
    │        ┌──────[100Ω 5W]──────┐                            │
    │        │                      │                            │
    │      PROBE                  PROBE                          │
    │       (+)                    (-)                           │
    │                                                            │
    │    Touch both terminals simultaneously                     │
    │    Hold for 10+ seconds                                    │
    │    Verify 0V with multimeter                               │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    BATTERY HAZARDS:                                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    LEAD-ACID BATTERIES:                                    │
    │    - Contain sulfuric acid (burns skin, eyes)              │
    │    - Produce hydrogen gas (explosive)                      │
    │    - Can deliver hundreds of amps (arcing, fire)           │
    │    - Heavy (dropping hazard)                               │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Wear eye protection and gloves                        │
    │    - Ensure ventilation (hydrogen dispersal)               │
    │    - No sparks/flames near batteries                       │
    │    - Connect/disconnect with power off                     │
    │    - Red = positive, Black = negative                      │
    │    - Connect positive first, disconnect last               │
    │    - Use insulated tools                                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    ACID SPILL RESPONSE:                                    │
    │    1. Neutralize with baking soda (sodium bicarbonate)     │
    │    2. Flush skin/eyes with water for 15+ minutes           │
    │    3. Seek medical attention for eye exposure              │
    │                                                            │
    │    LITHIUM BATTERIES:                                      │
    │    - Can catch fire if damaged, overcharged, shorted       │
    │    - Fire cannot be extinguished with water                │
    │    - Toxic fumes when burning                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Use appropriate charge controller                     │
    │    - Never puncture or crush                               │
    │    - Don't charge below freezing                           │
    │    - Store at 50% charge if long-term                      │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

12.3 MECHANICAL SAFETY

text

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ROTATING MACHINERY HAZARDS:                             │
    │                                                            │
    │    Rotating parts can:                                     │
    │    - Catch loose clothing, hair, jewelry                   │
    │    - Cause severe lacerations                              │
    │    - Break bones (high-speed/high-torque)                  │
    │    - Throw debris (failed components)                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Tie back long hair                                    │
    │    - Remove loose clothing, jewelry                        │
    │    - Keep hands away from rotating parts                   │
    │    - Install guards over exposed shafts/belts              │
    │    - Stop rotation before adjustments                      │
    │    - Wait for complete stop (coasting danger)              │
    │                                                            │
    │    GUARD DESIGN:                                           │
    │                                                            │
    │        ┌───────────────────────────────┐                   │
    │        │  ╭─────────────────────────╮  │                   │
    │        │  │      MESH/SOLID         │  │                   │
    │        │  │        GUARD            │  │                   │
    │        │  │    ┌───────────┐        │  │                   │
    │        │  │    │  ROTOR    │        │  │                   │
    │        │  │    └───────────┘        │  │                   │
    │        │  │                         │  │                   │
    │        │  ╰─────────────────────────╯  │                   │
    │        └───────────────────────────────┘                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    Openings must be small enough to prevent                │
    │    finger entry (<12mm) or far enough away                 │
    │    (>50mm from rotating parts)                             │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    STRUCTURAL FAILURE HAZARDS:                             │
    │                                                            │
    │    ROTOR FAILURE:                                          │
    │    If rotor disc fails at high speed:                      │
    │    - Magnets become projectiles                            │
    │    - Disc fragments fly outward                            │
    │    - Severe injury/death possible                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Use quality rotor material (no cracks/voids)          │
    │    - Secure magnets mechanically (not just glue)           │
    │    - Balance rotor (reduce vibration stress)               │
    │    - Limit maximum speed (governor or design limit)        │
    │    - Install burst containment (steel ring around rotor)   │
    │    - Don't operate cracked/damaged rotors                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    MAXIMUM SAFE SPEED:                                     │
    │    For wood/plastic rotors: Tip speed < 50 m/s            │
    │                                                            │
    │    Tip speed = π × Diameter × RPM / 60                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ Diameter │ Max Safe RPM │                             │
    │    │──────────│──────────────│                             │
    │    │  100mm   │    9500      │                             │
    │    │  200mm   │    4775      │                             │
    │    │  300mm   │    3180      │                             │
    │    │  400mm   │    2390      │                             │
    │    │  500mm   │    1910      │                             │
    │                                                            │
    │    DIY generators typically operate well below these       │
    │    limits (usually <1000 RPM)                              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    WIND TURBINE SPECIFIC HAZARDS:                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    - Blades spinning in high wind = extreme danger         │
    │    - Tower climbing = fall hazard                          │
    │    - Lightning strike risk                                 │
    │    - Blade throw in storm/overspeed                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Install brake/furling mechanism                       │
    │    - Never climb tower when wind present                   │
    │    - Install lightning protection                          │
    │    - Use guy wires and proper foundations                  │
    │    - Establish safety perimeter (blade length + 50%)       │
    │    - Regular blade inspection                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    SAFETY PERIMETER:                                       │
    │                                                            │
    │                    ╭──────────────╮                        │
    │                   ╱                ╲                       │
    │                  ╱                  ╲                      │
    │                 ╱        ┌┐          ╲                     │
    │                │         ├┤  TURBINE  │                    │
    │                │         └┘           │                    │
    │                 ╲                    ╱                     │
    │                  ╲                  ╱                      │
    │                   ╲                ╱                       │
    │                    ╰──────────────╯                        │
    │                                                            │
    │    No entry during operation                               │
    │    Radius = Blade length × 1.5                             │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    WATER TURBINE SPECIFIC HAZARDS:                         │
    │                                                            │
    │    - Drowning (working near water)                         │
    │    - Hypothermia (cold water exposure)                     │
    │    - Flash floods                                          │
    │    - Debris in water flow                                  │
    │    - Slippery surfaces                                     │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Wear life jacket near deep water                      │
    │    - Install upstream debris screen                        │
    │    - Monitor weather for flood conditions                  │
    │    - Use non-slip surfaces on walkways                     │
    │    - Never work alone near water                           │
    │    - Install emergency shutoff accessible from shore       │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

12.4 FIRE SAFETY

text

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FIRE CAUSES IN ELECTRICAL SYSTEMS:                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    1. Overloaded wires (too much current)                  │
    │    2. Loose connections (arcing)                           │
    │    3. Short circuits                                       │
    │    4. Failed components (overheating)                      │
    │    5. Battery thermal runaway                              │
    │    6. Flammable materials near hot components              │
    │                                                            │
    │    PREVENTION:                                             │
    │    - Use correctly sized wire for current                  │
    │    - Install fuses/breakers                                │
    │    - Keep connections tight                                │
    │    - Ensure ventilation around electronics                 │
    │    - Keep flammables away from generator                   │
    │    - Regular inspection for damage/wear                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    WIRE SIZING GUIDE (Maximum current):                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ AWG  │ Diameter │ Max Current (open air) │            │
    │    │──────│──────────│────────────────────────│            │
    │    │  24  │  0.5mm   │         3A             │            │
    │    │  22  │  0.6mm   │         5A             │            │
    │    │  20  │  0.8mm   │         7A             │            │
    │    │  18  │  1.0mm   │        10A             │            │
    │    │  16  │  1.3mm   │        15A             │            │
    │    │  14  │  1.6mm   │        20A             │            │
    │    │  12  │  2.0mm   │        25A             │            │
    │    │  10  │  2.6mm   │        35A             │            │
    │                                                            │
    │    Derate 20% if wires bundled together                    │
    │    Derate 30% if in enclosed space                         │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FIRE RESPONSE:                                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    ELECTRICAL FIRE:                                        │
    │    1. Disconnect power source if safely possible           │
    │    2. Use CO2 or dry powder extinguisher                   │
    │    3. DO NOT use water on energized equipment              │
    │    4. Evacuate if fire spreads                             │
    │    5. Call emergency services                              │
    │                                                            │
    │    BATTERY FIRE:                                           │
    │    Lead-acid: Water OK after power disconnected            │
    │    Lithium: Water may cause explosion                      │
    │             - Use dry powder, sand, or                     │
    │             - Let burn in controlled manner                │
    │             - Evacuate due to toxic fumes                  │
    │                                                            │
    │    FIRE EXTINGUISHER TYPES:                                │
    │                                                            │
    │    │ Type       │ Use On                   │ Electrical? │ │
    │    │────────────│──────────────────────────│─────────────│ │
    │    │ Water      │ Wood, paper, fabric      │     NO      │ │
    │    │ CO2        │ Electrical, liquids      │    YES      │ │
    │    │ Dry Powder │ All fires                │    YES      │ │
    │    │ Foam       │ Liquids                  │     NO      │ │
    │                                                            │
    │    RECOMMENDED: Keep dry powder extinguisher near          │
    │                 generator installation                     │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

12.5 PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT (PPE)

text

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    RECOMMENDED PPE BY TASK:                                │
    │                                                            │

    │    □ Safety glasses (shatter protection)                   │
    │    □ Heavy work gloves (crush protection)                  │

    │                                                            │

    │    □ Non-conductive footwear                              │
    │    □ No metal jewelry                                      │
    │                                                            │
    │    MECHANICAL CONSTRUCTION:                                │
    │    □ Safety glasses                                        │
    │    □ Work gloves                                           │
    │    □ Hearing protection (power tools)                      │
    │    □ Dust mask (cutting/sanding)                           │
    │    □ Steel-toe boots (heavy components)                    │
    │                                                            │
    │    BATTERY HANDLING:                                       │
    │    □ Safety glasses/face shield                            │
    │    □ Acid-resistant gloves                                 │
    │    □ Apron (acid spill protection)                         │
    │                                                            │
    │    WIND/WATER TURBINE WORK:                                │

    │                                                            │

12.6 EMERGENCY PROCEDURES

text

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    ELECTRIC SHOCK RESPONSE:                                │
    │                                                            │
    │    1. DO NOT touch victim if still in contact with source  │
    │    2. Disconnect power OR                                  │
    │    3. Use non-conductive object to separate victim         │
    │       (dry wood, plastic, rubber)                          │
    │    4. Check breathing and pulse                            │
    │    5. Begin CPR if needed and trained                      │
    │    6. Treat burns (cool water, cover)                      │
    │    7. Seek medical attention (even if seems OK -           │
    │       internal injuries possible)                          │
    │                                                            │
    │    ⚠️ Even minor shocks can cause:                         │
    │    - Delayed heart arrhythmia                              │
    │    - Internal burns                                        │
    │    - Muscle damage                                         │
    │    ALWAYS seek medical evaluation after shock              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    MAGNET INJURY RESPONSE:                                 │
    │                                                            │
    │    CRUSHING INJURY:                                        │
    │    1. Do not try to pull magnet off (may worsen injury)    │
    │    2. Slide magnet sideways to release                     │
    │    3. Apply pressure to stop bleeding                      │
    │    4. Clean wound, apply bandage                           │
    │    5. Seek medical attention for deep/severe injuries      │
    │                                                            │
    │    SWALLOWED MAGNET:                                       │
    │    ⚠️ MEDICAL EMERGENCY - especially if multiple magnets   │
    │    1. Do NOT induce vomiting                               │
    │    2. Go to emergency room IMMEDIATELY                     │
    │    3. Inform medical staff: magnets swallowed              │
    │    4. X-ray will locate magnets                            │
    │    5. Surgery may be required                              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    CHEMICAL EXPOSURE (Battery Acid):                       │
    │                                                            │
    │    SKIN CONTACT:                                           │
    │    1. Remove contaminated clothing                         │
    │    2. Flush with large amounts of water (15+ minutes)      │
    │    3. Neutralize remaining acid with baking soda paste     │
    │    4. Seek medical attention for burns                     │
    │                                                            │
    │    EYE CONTACT:                                            │
    │    1. Flush with water IMMEDIATELY (15+ minutes)           │
    │    2. Hold eyelids open during flushing                    │
    │    3. SEEK EMERGENCY MEDICAL ATTENTION                     │
    │    4. Continue flushing during transport                   │
    │                                                            │
    │    INHALATION (hydrogen gas, acid fumes):                  │
    │    1. Move to fresh air immediately                        │
    │    2. If breathing difficult, seek medical attention       │
    │    3. Administer oxygen if available and trained           │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │    FIRE EMERGENCY:                                         │
    │                                                            │
    │    1. Alert others (shout "FIRE")                          │
    │    2. If small and contained:                              │
    │       - Disconnect power if safe                           │
    │       - Use appropriate extinguisher                       │
    │       - Aim at base of flames                              │
    │       - Sweep side to side                                 │
    │    3. If spreading or cannot control:                      │
    │       - EVACUATE immediately                               │
    │       - Close doors behind you                             │
    │       - Call emergency services                            │
    │       - Do not re-enter                                    │
    │       - Meet at designated safe point                      │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

13.4 VOLTAGE/POWER RELATIONSHIPS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    FUNDAMENTAL FORMULAS:

    Power (W) = Voltage (V) × Current (A)
    
    P = V × I
    V = P / I
    I = P / V

    OHM'S LAW:
    
    V = I × R
    I = V / R
    R = V / I

    POWER AND RESISTANCE:
    
    P = I² × R
    P = V² / R

    EXAMPLE CALCULATIONS:

    │ Scenario                  │ Calculation              │
    │───────────────────────────│──────────────────────────│
    │ 12V, 5A load              │ P = 12 × 5 = 60W        │
    │ 100W load on 24V          │ I = 100/24 = 4.2A       │
    │ 10A at 50W                │ V = 50/10 = 5V          │
    │ 12V through 4Ω            │ I = 12/4 = 3A           │
    │ 3A through 4Ω             │ P = 3² × 4 = 36W        │

13.5 UNIT CONVERSIONS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    POWER:
    1 Watt = 1 Joule/second
    1 kilowatt (kW) = 1000 Watts
    1 horsepower (hp) = 746 Watts

    ENERGY:
    1 Watt-hour (Wh) = 3600 Joules
    1 kilowatt-hour (kWh) = 1000 Wh
    
    SPEED:
    1 RPM = 0.1047 rad/second
    Tip speed (m/s) = π × diameter(m) × RPM / 60

    LENGTH:
    1 inch = 25.4 mm
    1 foot = 304.8 mm
    1 meter = 39.37 inches

    TEMPERATURE:
    °C = (°F - 32) × 5/9
    °F = (°C × 9/5) + 32

13.6 COIL TURNS CALCULATOR
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    TARGET VOLTAGE ESTIMATION:

    For axial flux generator:
    
    V_peak ≈ 4 × N × B × A × RPM × Poles / 60
    
    Where:
    N = Number of turns per coil
    B = Magnetic flux density (~0.3-0.5 Tesla for NdFeB)
    A = Coil area facing magnet (m²)
    RPM = Rotations per minute
    Poles = Number of pole pairs (magnets/2)

    SIMPLIFIED ESTIMATION:

    For typical DIY generator with strong magnets:
    
    V_open ≈ 0.03 × Turns × Magnet_Width(cm) × RPM / 100
    
    Example: 100 turns, 3cm magnets, 200 RPM
    V_open ≈ 0.03 × 100 × 3 × 200/100 = 18V
    
    (Rough approximation - actual results vary)

    TURNS FOR TARGET VOLTAGE:

    │ Target V │ Magnet │ At 150 RPM │ At 300 RPM │
    │──────────│────────│────────────│────────────│
    │    5V    │  2cm   │  ~170 turns │  ~85 turns │
    │   12V    │  2cm   │  ~400 turns │ ~200 turns │
    │    5V    │  3cm   │  ~110 turns │  ~55 turns │
    │   12V    │  3cm   │  ~270 turns │ ~135 turns │
    │    5V    │  4cm   │   ~80 turns │  ~40 turns │
    │   12V    │  4cm   │  ~200 turns │ ~100 turns │

    These are estimates - always test and adjust!

13.7 BATTERY CAPACITY REFERENCE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    BATTERY CAPACITY = Amp-hours (Ah)
    
    Energy (Wh) = Voltage × Amp-hours
    
    │ Battery Type      │ Voltage │ Typical Ah │ Energy (Wh) │
    │───────────────────│─────────│────────────│─────────────│
    │ Car battery       │   12V   │   40-80    │  480-960    │
    │ Deep cycle (small)│   12V   │   50-100   │  600-1200   │
    │ Deep cycle (large)│   12V   │  100-200   │ 1200-2400   │
    │ Golf cart (6V)    │    6V   │  200-230   │ 1200-1380   │
    │ Phone battery     │   3.7V  │    2-5     │    7-19     │
    │ Laptop battery    │  10-14V │    4-8     │   40-100    │
    │ Power bank        │   3.7V  │   5-30     │   18-111    │
    │ E-bike battery    │  36-48V │   10-20    │  360-960    │

    RUN TIME CALCULATION:
    
    Hours = (Battery Ah × Battery V × Efficiency) / Load W
    
    Example: 100Ah 12V battery, 60W load, 85% efficiency
    Hours = (100 × 12 × 0.85) / 60 = 17 hours

    CHARGING TIME CALCULATION:
    
    Hours = (Battery Ah × 1.2) / Charge Current
    
    Example: 100Ah battery, 10A charger
    Hours = (100 × 1.2) / 10 = 12 hours

13.9 RPM ESTIMATION WITHOUT TACHOMETER
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    METHOD 1: Count revolutions
    
    1. Mark a point on rotor
    2. Count rotations for 15 seconds
    3. Multiply by 4 = RPM
    
    METHOD 2: Stroboscopic
    
    1. Use smartphone slow-motion camera
    2. Record at known frame rate (120fps, 240fps)
    3. Count frames per revolution
    4. RPM = (Frame rate / Frames per rev) × 60
    
    METHOD 3: Sound frequency
    
    1. Generator produces audible hum
    2. Record with phone
    3. Use audio spectrum analyzer app
    4. Find peak frequency
    5. RPM = (Frequency × 60) / Pole pairs

    TYPICAL RANGES:
    
    │ Drive Method      │ Typical RPM      │
    │───────────────────│──────────────────│
    │ Hand crank (slow) │    60-100        │
    │ Hand crank (fast) │   150-250        │
    │ Pedal (casual)    │   150-250        │
    │ Pedal (effort)    │   250-400        │
    │ Wind (light)      │   100-200        │
    │ Wind (moderate)   │   200-400        │
    │ Wind (strong)     │   400-800        │
    │ Water wheel       │    20-100        │
    │ Pelton turbine    │   200-1000       │

    MECHANICAL COMPONENTS:
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Qty │ Item                    │ Size/Spec        │ Source │
    │─────│─────────────────────────│──────────────────│────────│
    │ 1-3 │ Rotor disc(s)           │ 400mm Ø × 20mm   │ Ply/Alu│
    │ 2-4 │ Stator plates           │ 500mm Ø × 20mm   │ Plywood│
    │  2  │ Frame plates            │ 550×550×25mm     │ Ply/Stl│
    │  4  │ Frame uprights          │ Angle or tube    │ Steel  │
    │  4  │ Spacer rods             │ M12 × 150mm      │ Hardwr │
    │ 20+ │ Nuts, bolts, washers    │ M12, M10, M8     │ Hardwr │
    │  2  │ Bearings                │ 6202/6203        │ Indust │
    │  1  │ Shaft                   │ 15-20mm × 300mm  │ Steel  │
    │  2  │ Pillow block housings   │ Match bearings   │ Indust │
    │  1  │ Coupling                │ Flexible type    │ Indust │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    MAGNETIC COMPONENTS:
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Qty │ Item                    │ Size/Spec        │ Source │
    │─────│─────────────────────────│──────────────────│────────│
    │20-32│ Neodymium magnets       │ 40×20×10mm       │ Purchase│
    │     │ N42 grade or better     │ or equiv area    │ or Scav│
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    ELECTRICAL COMPONENTS:
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Qty │ Item                    │ Size/Spec        │ Source │
    │─────│─────────────────────────│──────────────────│────────│
    │300m+│ Copper wire             │ AWG 14-16        │ Purchase│
    │  1  │ Rectifier bridge        │ 50A 100V         │ Weldr  │
    │ 5+  │ Capacitors              │ 4700µF 63V       │ Indust │
    │  1  │ MPPT controller         │ 24/48V 30-40A    │ Solar  │
    │  4+ │ Batteries               │ 12V 100-200Ah    │ Auto   │
    │  1  │ DC breaker panel        │ 4-8 way          │ Electr │
    │  1  │ Main disconnect         │ 100A             │ Electr │
    │ 20m │ Battery cable           │ AWG 6-10         │ Auto   │
    │  1  │ Inverter (optional)     │ 1000W+ pure sine │ Solar  │
    │  1  │ Monitoring system       │ V, A, W display  │ Electr │
    │  1  │ Dump load + relay       │ Match power      │ Custom │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    WIND TURBINE OPTION:
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Qty │ Item                    │ Size/Spec        │ Source │
    │─────│─────────────────────────│──────────────────│────────│
    │  3  │ Blades                  │ 1-1.5m each      │ Wood   │
    │  1  │ Hub                     │ Steel plate      │ Custom │
    │  1  │ Tower pipe              │ 50-100mm, 3-6m   │ Pipe   │
    │  4  │ Guy wires               │ Steel cable      │ Hardwr │
    │  4  │ Ground anchors          │ Concrete/screw   │ Custom │
    │  1  │ Yaw bearing             │ Large/lazy susan │ Furn   │
