    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CHAPTER 15: STEP-BY-STEP BUILD GUIDE (TIER 1)

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
         COMPLETE STEP-BY-STEP BUILD GUIDE: TIER 1 GENERATOR
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

This chapter provides detailed instructions for building a 
complete TIER 1 generator (5-20W output).

ESTIMATED BUILD TIME: 4-6 hours
SKILL LEVEL: Beginner to Intermediate

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 1: GATHER MATERIALS AND TOOLS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Before starting, verify you have all materials from Section 14.2.

TOOLS NEEDED:
□ Saw (hand saw or jigsaw)
□ Drill with bits (8mm, 10mm, plus sizes for screws)
□ Screwdrivers (Phillips, flat)
□ Pliers
□ Wire strippers/cutters
□ File or sandpaper
□ Ruler/measuring tape
□ Compass or string (for circles)
□ Marker/pencil
□ Clamps (helpful)
□ Soldering iron (optional but recommended)
□ Multimeter (essential for testing)
□ Safety glasses
□ Work gloves

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 2: CUT ROTOR DISC
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

MATERIAL: 15mm plywood or HDPE plastic
SIZE: 150mm diameter

PROCEDURE:

1. Mark center point on material
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │               ●                     │ ← Mark center
   │             center                  │
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   └─────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Draw 150mm diameter circle
   - Use compass set to 75mm radius, OR
   - Nail at center, tie string 75mm long, attach pencil
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                     │
   │         ╭─────────────╮             │
   │        ╱               ╲            │
   │       │        ●        │           │
   │        ╲               ╱            │
   │         ╰─────────────╯             │
   │                                     │
   └─────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Cut circle
   - Jigsaw: Follow line directly
   - Hand saw: Cut octagon first, then round corners
   
4. Drill center hole
   - Mark center with punch or nail
   - Drill 8mm hole (match shaft diameter)
   - Keep drill perpendicular
   
5. Sand edges smooth
   - Round over any sharp edges
   - Ensure hole is clean

QUALITY CHECK:
□ Circle is round (within 2-3mm)
□ Center hole is centered (within 1mm)
□ Surface is flat (no warping)
□ Edges are smooth

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 3: MARK AND MOUNT MAGNETS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CONFIGURATION: 8 magnets at 45° intervals

1. Mark magnet positions

   Draw lines from center at 45° intervals:
   0°, 45°, 90°, 135°, 180°, 225°, 270°, 315°
   
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                           │
   │                   0°                      │
   │               ╱   │   ╲                   │
   │           315°    │    45°                │
   │             ╲     │     ╱                 │
   │    270° ─────────●───────── 90°           │
   │             ╱     │     ╲                 │
   │           225°    │    135°               │
   │               ╲   │   ╱                   │
   │                  180°                     │
   │                                           │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Mark magnet centers
   - Distance from center: 55mm (adjust for your magnet size)
   - Mark center of each magnet position on the lines
   
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                           │
   │                  [M1]                     │
   │              [M8]   [M2]                  │
   │                                           │
   │           [M7]   ●   [M3]                 │
   │                                           │
   │              [M6]   [M4]                  │
   │                  [M5]                     │
   │                                           │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Test magnet polarity
   - Take first magnet, mark one face as "UP"
   - Test each other magnet against it:
     - If repels: Same pole - mark "UP" on that face
     - If attracts: Opposite pole - mark other face "UP"
   
4. Arrange magnets on rotor (DO NOT GLUE YET)
   - Alternate: UP-DOWN-UP-DOWN-UP-DOWN-UP-DOWN
   
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                           │
   │                  [N↑]                     │
   │              [S↓]   [S↓]                  │
   │                                           │
   │           [N↑]   ●   [N↑]                 │
   │                                           │
   │              [S↓]   [S↓]                  │
   │                  [N↑]                     │
   │                                           │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

5. Verify polarity
   - Adjacent magnets should attract through rotor
   - If two adjacent magnets repel: Flip one
   
6. Glue magnets
   ⚠️ CAREFUL: Magnets will try to snap together!
   
   a. Clean rotor surface with sandpaper
   b. Mix 2-part epoxy
   c. Apply thin layer to magnet position
   d. Place magnet (correct polarity!)
   e. Press firmly for 30 seconds
   f. Move to next position (work opposite sides to avoid attraction)
   
   GLUING ORDER (minimizes magnetic interference):
   Position 1, then 5, then 3, then 7, then 2, then 6, then 4, then 8
   
7. Allow epoxy to cure (follow package directions, typically 24 hours)

8. Optional: Add mechanical retention
   - Wrap tape around rotor edges over magnets, OR
   - Add small screws/nails at magnet edges as stops

QUALITY CHECK:
□ All 8 magnets attached
□ Polarity alternates (N-S-N-S-N-S-N-S)
□ Magnets centered on marks
□ Magnets firmly attached (try to move each)
□ Rotor still balanced (no heavy side)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 4: CUT STATOR RINGS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

MATERIAL: 15mm plywood (non-magnetic)
SIZE: 200mm outer diameter, 100mm inner diameter

CUT TWO IDENTICAL RINGS

1. Mark outer circle (200mm diameter = 100mm radius)
2. Mark inner circle (100mm diameter = 50mm radius)

   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                           │
   │       ╭─────────────────────────╮         │
   │      ╱                           ╲        │
   │     ╱      ╭───────────────╮      ╲       │
   │    │      ╱                 ╲      │      │
   │    │     │                   │     │      │
   │    │     │        ●          │     │      │
   │    │     │                   │     │      │
   │    │      ╲                 ╱      │      │
   │     ╲      ╰───────────────╯      ╱       │
   │      ╲                           ╱        │
   │       ╰─────────────────────────╯         │
   │                                           │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Cut outer circle (same method as rotor)

4. Cut inner circle
   - Drill hole inside inner line
   - Insert jigsaw blade
   - Cut along inner line
   
   OR if no jigsaw:
   - Drill many holes around inner circle (touching)
   - Break out center
   - File smooth

5. Sand all edges smooth

6. Mark coil positions (12 positions at 30° intervals)
   - Offset from magnet positions by 15°
   - If magnets at 0°, 45°, 90°... 
     then coils at 15°, 45°, 75°, 105°...
   
   Actually for 8 magnets, 12 coils, use this pattern:
   - Coils every 30°: 0°, 30°, 60°, 90°, 120°, 150°, 180°, 210°, 240°, 270°, 300°, 330°
   - Or for simpler 8-coil version (matching magnets): 
     22.5°, 67.5°, 112.5°, 157.5°, 202.5°, 247.5°, 292.5°, 337.5°

   FOR THIS BUILD: Use 6 coils (simpler, 3-phase)
   - Coils at: 0°, 60°, 120°, 180°, 240°, 300°

QUALITY CHECK:
□ Two identical rings
□ Outer diameter correct (200mm ±3mm)
□ Inner diameter correct (100mm ±3mm)
□ Rings are flat
□ Edges smooth

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 5: WIND COILS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

SPECIFICATIONS:
- Number of coils: 6 (for 3-phase)
- Wire: AWG 20-22
- Turns per coil: 100 (adjust based on voltage target)
- Coil inner width: ~20mm (to straddle magnets)
- Coil outer width: ~40mm

1. Make winding form
   
   Cut cardboard or thin plywood:
   - Width = coil inner width (20mm)
   - Height = 50mm
   
   ┌──────────────┐
   │              │
   │              │  ← 50mm height
   │              │
   │              │
   └──────────────┘
       ↑
      20mm width

2. Wind first coil

   a. Leave 30cm lead at start (for connections)
   
   b. Wrap wire around form, counting turns:
   
      ┌──────────────┐
      │ ╔══════════╗ │
      │ ║██████████║ │  Wind tight, neat layers
      │ ║██████████║ │  Count: 10, 20, 30...
      │ ║██████████║ │
      │ ╚══════════╝ │
      └──────────────┘
   
   c. Keep tension consistent (not too tight, not loose)
   
   d. Build up layers evenly:
      Layer 1: Side to side
      Layer 2: Side to side (on top of layer 1)
      Continue until 100 turns reached
   
   e. Leave 30cm lead at end
   
   f. Wrap finished coil with tape to secure
   
   g. Carefully slide coil off form

3. Repeat for all 6 coils

   All coils should be identical:
   - Same number of turns
   - Same direction of winding
   - Same lead length

4. Test each coil
   - Measure resistance with multimeter
   - Should be similar for all coils (within 10%)
   - Typical: 1-5Ω depending on wire gauge and turns
   
   Record resistances:
   Coil 1: _____ Ω
   Coil 2: _____ Ω
   Coil 3: _____ Ω
   Coil 4: _____ Ω
   Coil 5: _____ Ω
   Coil 6: _____ Ω

QUALITY CHECK:
□ 6 identical coils
□ 100 turns each (or your target count)
□ Leads long enough (30cm each end)
□ Resistance measured and recorded
□ No damaged insulation

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 6: MOUNT COILS ON STATORS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CONFIGURATION: 6 coils total, 3 per stator (opposite sides)

WIRING: 3-phase star connection

1. Position coils on stator rings

   For 6 coils, 2 per phase, phases 120° apart:
   
   STATOR 1 (3 coils):
   - Phase A coil at 0°
   - Phase B coil at 120°
   - Phase C coil at 240°
   
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                           │
   │                 [A]                       │
   │                  0°                       │
   │                                           │
   │                                           │
   │       [C]                      [B]        │
   │       240°                    120°        │
   │                                           │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

   STATOR 2 (3 coils):
   - Phase A coil at 180° (opposite first A coil)
   - Phase B coil at 300° (opposite first B coil)
   - Phase C coil at 60° (opposite first C coil)
   
2. Route coil wires through stator

   Drill small holes for wire entry/exit:
   
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │       ○ ← wire hole                       │
   │     ┌─────┐                               │
   │     │COIL │                               │
   │     └─────┘                               │
   │       ○ ← wire hole                       │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Secure coils to stator

   Option A: Epoxy (permanent)
   - Apply epoxy under coil
   - Press coil in position
   - Allow to cure
   
   Option B: Tape + Screws (removable)
   - Tape coils down
   - Add small screws at corners as retainers
   
   Option C: Tie-wire (simple)
   - Drill holes either side of coil
   - Thread wire through, around coil, back through
   - Twist tight on back side

4. Connect coils (3-phase star)

   WIRING DIAGRAM:
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                             │
   │   Stator 1:          Stator 2:          Connections:        │
   │                                                             │
   │   [A1]●────────────────────────●[A2]                        │
   │       │                        │                            │
   │       └────────┬───────────────┘                            │
   │                │                                            │
   │            Phase A OUT ─────────────→ To Rectifier          │
   │                                                             │
   │   [B1]●────────────────────────●[B2]                        │
   │       │                        │                            │
   │       └────────┬───────────────┘                            │
   │                │                                            │
   │            Phase B OUT ─────────────→ To Rectifier          │
   │                                                             │
   │   [C1]●────────────────────────●[C2]                        │
   │       │                        │                            │
   │       └────────┬───────────────┘                            │
   │                │                                            │
   │            Phase C OUT ─────────────→ To Rectifier          │
   │                                                             │
   │   Other ends of all coils connect together = STAR POINT    │
   │   (Can remain unconnected or connect to neutral)            │
   │                                                             │
   │   Coil series connection (within each phase):               │
   │   A1 END → A2 START (connected)                             │
   │   A1 START = Phase A output                                 │
   │   A2 END → Star point                                       │
   │   (Same for B and C phases)                                 │
   │                                                             │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   DETAILED COIL CONNECTION:
   
   For each phase (A, B, C):
   1. Connect END of Stator1 coil to START of Stator2 coil
   2. The remaining START wire = Phase Output
   3. The remaining END wire connects to Star Point
   
   STAR POINT: All three "END" wires tied together
   
5. Solder or crimp all connections

6. Insulate all connections with tape or heat shrink

7. Label wires: A, B, C (for rectifier connection)

QUALITY CHECK:
□ Coils firmly attached
□ Coils in correct positions
□ Wires routed and secured
□ All connections made per diagram
□ Connections insulated
□ No shorts (test with multimeter)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 7: BUILD FRAME
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

FRAME TYPE: H-Frame (simple, effective)

MATERIAL: 
- 2× Frame plates: 250×250×18mm plywood
- 4× Spacer rods: M8 × 80mm threaded rod (or bolts)
- 8× Nuts: M8
- 8× Washers: M8

1. Cut frame plates (if not pre-cut)
   - 2 pieces, 250mm × 250mm × 18mm

2. Mark bearing holes
   - Find center of each plate
   - Mark center point
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │               ●                     │
   │             center                  │
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   └─────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Mark spacer rod holes
   - At corners, 30mm from each edge
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
   │   ○                           ○     │  ← Spacer holes
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │               ●                     │  ← Bearing hole
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │                                     │
   │   ○                           ○     │  ← Spacer holes
   └─────────────────────────────────────┘

4. CRITICAL: Align plates for drilling
   - Stack both plates precisely aligned
   - Clamp together
   - Mark through both simultaneously

5. Drill bearing holes
   - Hole size = bearing outer diameter (22mm for 608 bearing)
   - Drill through both plates together
   - This ensures alignment

6. Drill spacer rod holes
   - Hole size = rod diameter + 1mm (9mm for M8)
   - Drill through both plates together

7. Install bearings
   - Press or tap bearings into holes
   - Should be snug fit (tight)
   - If loose: Add epoxy around bearing OR
     Wrap bearing with tape to increase diameter
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                     │
   │              ┌──────┐               │
   │              │BEARING│              │
   │              │  ┌─┐  │              │
   │              │  │●│  │ ← Shaft hole │
   │              │  └─┘  │              │
   │              └──────┘               │
   │                                     │
   └─────────────────────────────────────┘

8. Test bearing fit
   - Insert shaft through bearings (both plates)
   - Should rotate smoothly
   - No excessive play

QUALITY CHECK:
□ Both plates identical and aligned
□ Bearings installed and secure
□ Bearings aligned (shaft passes through both)
□ Shaft rotates freely in bearings
□ Spacer holes line up between plates

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 8: FINAL ASSEMBLY
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

1. Prepare shaft
   - Cut to length: ~150mm
   - Clean and deburr ends
   - Insert through one bearing in first plate

2. Assembly sequence

   a. First frame plate (flat on table, bearing up)
   
   b. Insert spacer rods (do not tighten yet)
   
   c. Place first stator on spacers (coils facing up)
   
   d. Add shim spacers for air gap (3-5mm thick)
      - Cardboard, washers, or stacked coins
      - Place at 3-4 points around circle
   
   e. Insert shaft through first stator center
   
   f. Place rotor on shaft (magnets facing up toward coils)
      - Temporarily hold in position
      - DO NOT let magnets contact stator!
   
   g. Add shim spacers on top of rotor (same thickness)
   
   h. Place second stator (coils facing down toward rotor)
   
   i. Insert shaft through second stator
   
   j. Lower second frame plate onto spacer rods
   
   k. Insert shaft through second bearing

3. Adjust air gaps
   
   - Temporarily 

     AIR GAP TARGET: 3-5mm on each side of rotor

   PROCEDURE:

   a. With shim spacers in place, hand-tighten spacer rod nuts
   
   b. Check gaps are even around entire circumference
      - Measure at 4 points: top, bottom, left, right
      - All should be equal (within 1mm)
   
   c. If uneven:
      - Loosen nuts
      - Adjust shimming
      - Re-tighten
      - Re-check
   
   d. Secure shaft to rotor
      - Mark shaft position on rotor
      - Remove rotor temporarily
      - Drill through rotor hub into shaft
      - Insert pin (nail, roll pin) OR
      - Use set screw if prepared
   
   e. Reinstall rotor on shaft in correct position
   
   f. Add shaft collars to prevent axial movement
   
      ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
      │                                                         │
      │   SHAFT COLLAR PLACEMENT:                               │
      │                                                         │
      │   ═══╬═══════════════════════════════════════╬═══       │
      │      ↑         ROTOR IN MIDDLE             ↑           │
      │    COLLAR                                COLLAR         │
      │   (prevents                            (prevents        │
      │    left shift)                         right shift)     │
      │                                                         │
      └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   
   g. Tighten all spacer rod nuts firmly

4. Final rotation check

   a. Turn shaft by hand - should rotate freely
   
   b. Listen for any rubbing sounds
      - If rubbing: Stop immediately
      - Loosen, adjust gaps, retighten
   
   c. Check for smooth, consistent rotation
      - No catching or cogging (slight is normal with magnets)
      - No vibration
   
   d. Spin freely and let coast
      - Should spin for several seconds
      - Quick stop = friction problem

5. Remove air gap shims

   a. Carefully slide shims out
   b. Verify rotor still centered
   c. Final rotation test

6. Secure stators

   a. If not already secured, add screws through stators
      into spacer positions or frame
   
   b. Stators must not move when operating

ASSEMBLY COMPLETE CHECKLIST:
□ Rotor centered between stators
□ Air gaps even (3-5mm each side)
□ Rotor secured to shaft
□ Shaft collars prevent axial movement
□ All nuts tightened
□ Rotates freely by hand
□ No rubbing or catching
□ Stators secured

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 9: WIRE ROUTING AND PROTECTION
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

1. Route stator wires out of frame

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                             │
   │   WIRE ROUTING:                                             │
   │                                                             │
   │        ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐          │
   │        │                                         │          │
   │        │    ┌───────────────────────────────┐    │          │
   │        │    │                               │    │          │
   │        │    │        GENERATOR              │    │          │
   │        │    │                               │    │          │
   │        │    └───────────────────────────────┘    │          │
   │        │              │ │ │                      │          │
   │        │              │ │ │ ← Wires routed       │          │
   │        │              │ │ │   through slot       │          │
   │        └──────────────┴─┴─┴──────────────────────┘          │
   │                       │ │ │                                 │
   │                       A B C ← Phase outputs                 │
   │                                                             │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Protect wires from damage

   a. Use grommets or tape at any sharp edges
   b. Secure wires to frame with cable ties or tape
   c. Ensure no wire can contact rotating parts
   d. Leave service loop for maintenance access

3. Label all wires

   - Phase A (red or A marking)
   - Phase B (yellow or B marking)
   - Phase C (blue or C marking)
   - Neutral/Star (if connected out)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 10: BUILD RECTIFIER CIRCUIT
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

3-PHASE BRIDGE RECTIFIER

COMPONENTS NEEDED:
- 6× Diodes (1N5400 series, 3A minimum)
- 1× Mounting board (wood, plastic, or perfboard)
- Wire for connections
- Solder (if soldering) or wire nuts

CIRCUIT SCHEMATIC:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   3-PHASE FULL-WAVE BRIDGE RECTIFIER                        │
    │                                                             │
    │                ┌──────[D1]──────┐                           │
    │   Phase A ─────┤                ├────┐                      │
    │                └──────[D4]──────┘    │                      │
    │                        │             │                      │
    │                ┌──────[D2]──────┐    │                      │
    │   Phase B ─────┤                ├────┼──── DC+ (Positive)   │
    │                └──────[D5]──────┘    │                      │
    │                        │             │                      │
    │                ┌──────[D3]──────┐    │                      │
    │   Phase C ─────┤                ├────┘                      │
    │                └──────[D6]──────┘                           │
    │                        │                                    │
    │                        │                                    │
    │                        └─────────────── DC- (Negative/GND)  │
    │                                                             │
    │   D1, D2, D3 = Upper diodes (cathode to DC+)               │
    │   D4, D5, D6 = Lower diodes (anode to DC-)                 │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PHYSICAL LAYOUT:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   MOUNTING BOARD LAYOUT:                                    │
    │                                                             │
    │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │   │                                                     │   │
    │   │     ┌───┐     ┌───┐     ┌───┐                      │   │
    │   │     │D1 │     │D2 │     │D3 │  ← Upper row         │   │
    │   │     │ ▼ │     │ ▼ │     │ ▼ │    (all cathodes     │   │
    │   │     └─┬─┘     └─┬─┘     └─┬─┘     connected)        │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │   ════╪═════════╪═════════╪══════════► DC+         │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │       ●         ●         ●    ← Input points       │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │       A         B         C    ← From generator     │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │       ●         ●         ●                         │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │     ┌─┴─┐     ┌─┴─┐     ┌─┴─┐                      │   │
    │   │     │ ▲ │     │ ▲ │     │ ▲ │  ← Lower row         │   │
    │   │     │D4 │     │D5 │     │D6 │    (all anodes       │   │
    │   │     └───┘     └───┘     └───┘     connected)        │   │
    │   │       │         │         │                         │   │
    │   │   ════╪═════════╪═════════╪══════════► DC-         │   │
    │   │                                                     │   │
    │   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CONSTRUCTION STEPS:

1. Identify diode polarity
   
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                             │
   │   DIODE IDENTIFICATION:                                     │
   │                                                             │
   │       ┌─────────────────────────────┐                       │
   │       │█████████████████████      │█│                       │
   │       └─────────────────────────────┘                       │
   │        ↑                          ↑                         │
   │      ANODE                     CATHODE                      │
   │    (no band)                  (band end)                    │
   │                                                             │
   │   Current flows: Anode → Cathode (in direction of arrow)   │
   │                                                             │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Mount diodes on board
   - Screw terminals, solder, or wire-wrap connections
   - Keep diodes separated (heat dissipation)
   - Add heatsink if expecting >1A continuous

3. Wire upper diodes (D1, D2, D3)
   - Connect all three CATHODES together
   - This junction = DC+ output

4. Wire lower diodes (D4, D5, D6)
   - Connect all three ANODES together
   - This junction = DC- output

5. Wire phase inputs
   - Phase A → Junction of D1 anode and D4 cathode
   - Phase B → Junction of D2 anode and D5 cathode
   - Phase C → Junction of D3 anode and D6 cathode

6. Add output wires
   - DC+ (red wire)
   - DC- (black wire)

7. Test rectifier before connecting

   DIODE TEST (Multimeter in diode mode):
   
   │ Test Points          │ Expected Reading    │
   │──────────────────────│─────────────────────│
   │ D1 Anode → Cathode   │ 0.5-0.7V (forward)  │
   │ D1 Cathode → Anode   │ OL (open)           │
   │ (Repeat for D2-D6)   │                     │
   
   If any diode shows 0 both ways = shorted (replace)
   If any diode shows OL both ways = open (replace)

QUALITY CHECK:
□ All 6 diodes tested good
□ Upper cathodes connected together (DC+)
□ Lower anodes connected together (DC-)
□ Phase inputs at correct junctions
□ No short between DC+ and DC-
□ All connections secure and insulated

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 11: BUILD FILTER AND PROTECTION CIRCUIT
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

COMPLETE OUTPUT CIRCUIT:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   FROM         FUSE      FILTER        OUTPUT               │
    │   RECTIFIER                                                 │
    │                                                             │
    │   DC+ ────[FUSE]────┬────────┬─────────────── OUT+          │
    │            5A       │        │                              │
    │                   ──┴──    ──┴──                            │
    │                   2200µF   2200µF  ← Capacitors             │
    │                   ──┬──    ──┬──     in parallel            │
    │                     │        │                              │
    │   DC- ──────────────┴────────┴─────────────── OUT-          │
    │                                                             │
    │   OPTIONAL ADDITIONS:                                       │
    │                                                             │
    │   - LED indicator with 1kΩ resistor (shows power)          │
    │   - Voltmeter (shows output voltage)                        │
    │   - Blocking diode (prevents backfeed from battery)         │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

COMPONENT SELECTION:

FUSE:
- Rating: 125-150% of maximum expected current
- For 5A system: Use 7.5A or 10A fuse
- Type: Automotive blade fuse (easy to find, replace)
- Install in fuse holder for easy access

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   FUSE HOLDER WIRING:                                       │
    │                                                             │
    │        IN ──────┤ ○────┼────○ ├────── OUT                   │
    │                 │      │      │                             │
    │                 │   [FUSE]    │                             │
    │                 │      │      │                             │
    │                 └──────┴──────┘                             │
    │                    HOLDER                                   │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CAPACITORS:
- Electrolytic type
- Voltage rating: At least 1.5× expected maximum DC voltage
- For 12V system: Use 25V or 35V rated capacitors
- For 24V system: Use 50V rated capacitors
- Capacitance: 2000-4700µF per amp of output
- Multiple capacitors in parallel = additive capacitance

    ⚠️ CAPACITOR POLARITY:
    
    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   ELECTROLYTIC CAPACITOR:                                   │
    │                                                             │
    │        ┌─────────────────┐                                  │
    │        │    ─────────    │                                  │
    │        │   │       │ ←── │ Stripe indicates NEGATIVE (-)    │
    │      + │   │       │     │                                  │
    │        │   │       │     │                                  │
    │        │    ─────────    │                                  │
    │        └───────┬─────────┘                                  │
    │              ─ │ +                                          │
    │            NEGATIVE                                         │
    │            (shorter lead)                                   │
    │                                                             │
    │   ⚠️ REVERSED POLARITY = EXPLOSION HAZARD                   │
    │      Always verify + to DC+, - to DC-                       │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

LED INDICATOR (Optional):
- Shows power is present
- Helps troubleshooting
- Visible confirmation of generation

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   LED INDICATOR CIRCUIT:                                    │
    │                                                             │
    │   DC+ ────[1kΩ]────[LED]──── DC-                           │
    │                      ↓                                      │
    │                   (flat side                                │
    │                    is cathode,                              │
    │                    goes to -)                               │
    │                                                             │
    │   RESISTOR CALCULATION:                                     │
    │   R = (V_supply - V_LED) / I_LED                           │
    │   R = (12V - 2V) / 0.010A = 1000Ω = 1kΩ                   │
    │                                                             │
    │   For different voltages:                                   │
    │   │ Supply V │ Resistor │                                   │
    │   │    6V    │   390Ω   │                                   │
    │   │   12V    │   1kΩ    │                                   │
    │   │   24V    │   2.2kΩ  │                                   │
    │                                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CONSTRUCTION:

1. Mount components on board
   - Fuse holder
   - Capacitors (observe polarity!)
   - LED circuit if included
   - Terminal blocks for connections

2. Wire according to schematic

3. Label terminals clearly:
   - "FROM RECTIFIER +"
   - "FROM RECTIFIER -"
   - "TO LOAD/BATTERY +"
   - "TO LOAD/BATTERY -"

QUALITY CHECK:
□ Fuse installed and correct rating
□ Capacitor polarity correct
□ All connections secure
□ No shorts between + and -
□ LED lights when power applied (if installed)

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 12: SYSTEM INTEGRATION
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CONNECT ALL COMPONENTS:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   COMPLETE SYSTEM SCHEMATIC:                                │
    │                                                             │
    │   ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────┐    ┌─────────────────────┐  │
    │   │          │ A  │           │    │                     │  │
    │   │          ├────┤           │    │  ┌──────┐           │  │
    │   │ GENERATOR│ B  │ RECTIFIER ├────┤  │ FUSE │           │  │
    │   │          ├────┤  (6 diode)│ +  │  └──┬───┘           │  │
    │   │          │ C  │           │    │     │    ┌────────┐ │  │
    │   │          ├────┤           │    │  ───┴─── │ CHARGE │ │  │
    │   └──────────┘    └─────┬─────┘    │  CAPS    │CONTRLR │ │  │
    │                         │ -        │  ───┬─── │        │ │  │
    │                         │          │     │    └───┬────┘ │  │
    │                         └──────────┴─────┴────────┤      │  │
    │                                                   │      │  │
    │                                              ┌────┴────┐ │  │
    │                                              │ BATTERY │ │  │
    │                                              └─────────┘ │  │
    │                                                          │  │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CONNECTION SEQUENCE:

1. Connect generator to rectifier
   - Phase A → Rectifier input A
   - Phase B → Rectifier input B
   - Phase C → Rectifier input C

2. Connect rectifier to filter
   - Rectifier DC+ → Fuse → Filter DC+
   - Rectifier DC- → Filter DC-

3. Connect filter to charge controller (if using battery)
   - Filter output → Charge controller input
   - Follow charge controller instructions for battery connection

4. OR connect filter directly to load (if no battery)
   - Filter output → Load
   - ⚠️ Voltage will vary with speed!

WIRE SIZING:

    │ Current (Amps) │ Minimum Wire Gauge │
    │────────────────│────────────────────│
    │     1-3        │       AWG 20       │
    │     3-5        │       AWG 18       │
    │     5-10       │       AWG 16       │
    │    10-15       │       AWG 14       │
    │    15-20       │       AWG 12       │

Use appropriately sized wire for expected current levels.

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 13: INITIAL TESTING
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

⚠️ SAFETY FIRST:
- Have fire extinguisher nearby
- Wear safety glasses
- Work in ventilated area
- Don't touch electrical connections while running
- Have helper if possible

TEST SEQUENCE:

1. VISUAL INSPECTION
   
   □ All mechanical components secure
   □ All electrical connections tight
   □ No loose wires near rotating parts
   □ No obvious shorts or exposed conductors
   □ Fuse installed

2. NO-LOAD ROTATION TEST
   
   a. Disconnect all electrical loads
   b. Rotate shaft slowly by hand
   c. Verify smooth rotation
   d. Listen for any abnormal sounds
   
   EXPECTED: Smooth rotation with slight magnetic cogging

3. AC VOLTAGE TEST
   
   a. Set multimeter to AC Voltage
   b. Connect to any two phase outputs (before rectifier)
   c. Rotate shaft at moderate speed (~100-150 RPM)
   d. Read voltage
   
   EXPECTED: 5-15V AC (varies with speed and build)
   
   RECORD: _____ V AC at _____ RPM (estimated)

4. ALL PHASES TEST
   
   Measure between each phase pair:
   - A to B: _____ V AC
   - B to C: _____ V AC
   - C to A: _____ V AC
   
   EXPECTED: All three readings similar (within 20%)
   
   If one reading significantly different:
   → Check that phase's coil connections
   → Check for broken wire
   → Check coil resistance

5. DC OUTPUT TEST
   
   a. Set multimeter to DC Voltage
   b. Connect to filter output (+ and -)
   c. Rotate shaft at moderate speed
   d. Read voltage
   
   EXPECTED: ~1.4× the AC voltage reading
   
   Example: 10V AC → ~14V DC
   
   RECORD: _____ V DC at _____ RPM

6. POLARITY VERIFICATION
   
   a. Verify DC+ output is positive (red probe on +)
   b. Verify DC- output is negative (black probe on -)
   
   If reversed: Check rectifier wiring or swap output leads

7. LOAD TEST (Small Load)
   
   a. Connect small test load:
      - LED with resistor, OR
      - Small 12V bulb (if voltage sufficient), OR
      - USB phone charger module
   
   b. Rotate shaft
   
   c. Observe load operation:
      - LED should light
      - Brightness should increase with speed
   
   EXPECTED: Load operates when voltage exceeds minimum requirement

8. CURRENT TEST
   
   a. Set multimeter to DC Amps
   b. Connect in series with load
   c. Rotate at consistent speed
   d. Read current
   
   RECORD: _____ A at _____ V at _____ RPM
   
   Calculate power: P = V × I = _____ W

TEST RESULTS LOG:

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

    │   GENERATOR TEST LOG                                        │

    │                                                             │

    │   □ Abnormal sounds: YES / NO                               │

    │   │   100     │              │               │             │

    │   │           │      │          │       │                  │

STEP 14: ADD DRIVE MECHANISM
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

OPTION A: HAND CRANK (Simplest)

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                             │
    │   HAND CRANK CONSTRUCTION:                                  │
    │                                                             │
    │                 HANDLE                                      │
    │                   │                                         │
    │                   ●───────────────── ARM (150-200mm)        │
    │                                       │                     │
    │                                       │                     │
    │                                       ●──── OFFSET (30mm)   │
    │                                       │                     │
    │                                    ┌──┴──┐                  │

    │                                    │     │                  │
    │                                    └─────┘                  │
    │                                                             │
    │   MATERIALS:                                                │
    │   - Arm: Metal strip 25mm × 3mm × 200mm                    │
    │   - Handle: Wood dowel or pipe 20mm × 50mm                 │
    │   - Offset piece: Metal or wood 30mm                       │
    │                                                             │
    │   CONSTRUCTION:                                             │
    │   1. Drill hole in arm end for shaft (tight fit or pinned) │
    │   2. Drill hole in other end for handle                    │
    │   3. Handle should rotate freely (bearing or loose fit)    │
    │   4. Attach arm to shaft with set screw, pin, or weld      │

    ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

    │        PEDAL           BELT           GENERATOR             │

    │                                                             │
    │        ╭───╮                           ╭─╮                  │

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
STEP 15: FINAL COMMISSIONING
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

1. COMPLETE SYSTEM TEST

   a. Connect complete system:
      Generator → Rectifier → Filter → Charge Controller → Battery
      OR
      Generator → Rectifier → Filter → Voltage Regulator → Load

   b. Operate at intended speed

   c. Monitor:
      - Voltage (should match target: 12V, 24V, etc.)
      - Current (should not exceed ratings)
      - Temperature (components should stay cool to warm)

   d. Run for 15-30 minutes continuous

2. PERFORMANCE VERIFICATION

   Record actual performance:
   
   │ Condition         │ Voltage │ Current │ Power │
   │───────────────────│─────────│─────────│───────│
   │ No load           │         │    0    │   0   │
   │ Phone charging    │         │         │       │
   │ LED lights (3×5W) │         │         │       │
   │ Maximum tested    │         │         │       │

3. SAFETY CHECK

   □ No overheating components
   □ No unusual smells
   □ No sparking
   □ No excessive vibration
   □ All guards in place
   □ Emergency stop accessible (disconnect)

4. DOCUMENTATION

   Complete generator label:

   │                                                             │

   │                                                             │

   │   - Wire: AWG ___                                          │

   │   Performance:                                              │
   │   - Output type: 3-phase AC → DC                           │
   │   - Voltage at ___ RPM: ___ V DC                           │
   │   - Maximum current: ___ A                                  │
   │   - Maximum power: ___ W                                    │
   │                                                             │
   │   WARNINGS:                                                 │
   │   - Contains strong magnets                                 │
   │   - Keep away from electronics                              │
   │   - Do not exceed ___ RPM                                   │
   │                                                             │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

COMMISSIONING COMPLETE CHECKLIST:

□ All tests passed
□ Performance documented
□ Safety verified
□ Label attached
□ User trained (if not self)
□ Maintenance schedule established
□ Spare parts list created

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
             TIER 1 BUILD COMPLETE - GENERATOR OPERATIONAL
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

CHAPTER 16: OPTIMIZATION AND UPGRADES

text

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    OPTIMIZATION AND UPGRADES
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

16.1 IMPROVING OUTPUT VOLTAGE
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

IF VOLTAGE IS TOO LOW:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Option           │ Difficulty │ Improvement │ Notes        │
    │──────────────────│────────────│─────────────│──────────────│
    │ Add more turns   │   Medium   │    High     │ Rewind coils │
    │ Reduce air gap   │    Easy    │   Medium    │ Min 3mm safe │
    │ Stronger magnets │   Medium   │    High     │ May need new │
    │ Increase speed   │    Easy    │    High     │ Gearing/drive│
    │ Series coil conn │   Medium   │   Medium    │ Rewire only  │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

ADDING TURNS TO EXISTING COILS:

    If space permits, wind additional turns on top of existing:
    
    BEFORE:                    AFTER:
    ┌──────────────┐          ┌──────────────┐
    │ ╔══════════╗ │          │ ╔══════════╗ │
    │ ║██████████║ │    →     │ ║██████████║ │
    │ ║██████████║ │          │ ║██████████║ │
    │ ╚══════════╝ │          │ ║██████████║ │
    └──────────────┘          │ ╚══════════╝ │
                              └──────────────┘
        100 turns                 150 turns
        
    NOTE: More turns = more resistance = slightly less current
          Best for low-current, higher-voltage needs

REDUCING AIR GAP:

    Current gap        →    Reduced gap
    ┌─────┐ 10mm ┌────┐    ┌─────┐ 5mm ┌────┐
    │COILS│      │MAGS│    │COILS│     │MAGS│
    └─────┘      └────┘    └─────┘     └────┘
    
    PROCEDURE:
    1. Loosen frame nuts
    2. Remove some spacer material
    3. Re-tighten evenly
    4. Verify no rubbing
    5. Test improvement
    
    ⚠️ CAUTION: Too small gap → rotor contacts stator → damage
       Keep minimum 3mm gap for safety

16.2 IMPROVING OUTPUT CURRENT
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

IF CURRENT CAPACITY IS TOO LOW:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Option              │ Difficulty │ Improvement │ Notes     │
    │─────────────────────│────────────│─────────────│───────────│
    │ Use thicker wire    │    High    │    High     │ Rewind    │
    │ Parallel coil conn  │   Medium   │   Medium    │ Rewire    │
    │ Add more magnets    │   Medium   │   Medium    │ Modify    │
    │ Reduce resistance   │    Easy    │    Low      │ Connect.  │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PARALLEL vs SERIES CONNECTION:

    SERIES (Current limited by single coil):
    
    ─────[COIL1]────[COIL2]────[COIL3]────[COIL4]─────
    
    Voltage = Sum of all coils
    Current = Single coil current (limited by thin wire)
    
    PARALLEL (Current is sum of all coils):
    
    ─────┬─────[COIL1]─────┬─────
         │                 │
         ├─────[COIL2]─────┤
         │                 │
         ├─────[COIL3]─────┤
         │                 │
         └─────[COIL4]─────┘
    
    Voltage = Single coil voltage
    Current = Sum of all coil currents
    
    SERIES-PARALLEL (Balanced):
    
    ─────┬─────[COIL1]────[COIL2]─────┬─────
         │                            │
         └─────[COIL3]────[COIL4]─────┘
    
    Voltage = 2× single coil voltage
    Current = 2× single coil current

IMPROVING CONNECTIONS (Reducing resistance):

    1. Solder all connections (don't just twist)
    2. Use larger wire for main runs
    3. Shorten wire lengths where possible
    4. Clean and tighten all terminals
    5. Use proper crimped terminals

16.3 IMPROVING EFFICIENCY
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

SOURCES OF LOSS:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   POWER FLOW WITH LOSSES:                                  │
    │                                                            │
    │   MECHANICAL      MAGNETIC        ELECTRICAL               │
    │   INPUT           CONVERSION      OUTPUT                   │
    │      │                │              │                     │
    │      ▼                ▼              ▼                     │
    │   100% ──────────→ 90% ──────────→ 80% ──────→ USEFUL     │
    │                                                            │
    │      │                │              │                     │
    │      ▼                ▼              ▼                     │
    │   [Friction]     [Eddy current] [Wire resistance]          │
    │   [Bearing drag]  [Hysteresis]  [Diode drops]             │
    │   [Air drag]     [Flux leakage]  [Connection R]            │
    │                                                            │
    │   TYPICAL DIY GENERATOR: 50-70% overall efficiency         │
    │   OPTIMIZED DIY: 65-75%                                    │
    │   COMMERCIAL: 80-95%                                       │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

REDUCING MECHANICAL LOSSES:

    1. Use quality ball bearings (not bushings)
    2. Ensure bearings properly lubricated
    3. Balance rotor (reduce vibration)
    4. Align shaft precisely
    5. Minimize air gap (but safely)

REDUCING MAGNETIC LOSSES:

    1. Use non-conductive stator (wood, plastic) - no eddy currents
    2. If using metal stator, use laminated steel
    3. Maximize magnet-to-coil flux coupling
    4. Minimize flux leakage (proper magnet arrangement)

REDUCING ELECTRICAL LOSSES:

    1. Use thickest practical wire gauge
    2. Minimize connection resistance
    3. Use Schottky diodes (lower voltage drop)
    4. Shorten wiring runs
    5. Use proper wire sizing throughout

16.4 SCALING UP (From Tier 1 to Tier 2)
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

UPGRADE PATH:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   TIER 1 (5-20W)          →          TIER 2 (20-100W)     │
    │                                                            │
    │   8 magnets               →          12-16 magnets        │
    │   6 coils                 →          12 coils             │
    │   150mm rotor             →          250mm rotor          │
    │   AWG 20-22 wire          →          AWG 16-18 wire       │
    │   608 bearings            →          6001/6002 bearings   │
    │   Hand crank capable      →          Pedal/wind/water     │
    │                                                            │
    │   KEY CHANGES:                                             │
    │   • Larger diameter = more magnet area                    │
    │   • More magnets = smoother output                        │
    │   • Thicker wire = higher current capacity                │
    │   • Bigger bearings = longer life under load              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

KEEPING EXISTING COMPONENTS:

    If upgrading, you can often reuse:
    □ Rectifier (if rated for higher current)
    □ Capacitors (if voltage rating sufficient)
    □ Charge controller (if current rating sufficient)
    □ Wiring (if gauge appropriate)
    □ Some frame components
    
    Must replace:
    □ Rotor disc (larger)
    □ Stator rings (larger)
    □ Coils (more of them)
    □ Magnets (more of them)
    □ Bearings (heavier duty)
    □ Shaft (possibly larger)

16.5 ADDING MONITORING
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

USEFUL MONITORING ADDITIONS:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   COMPLETE MONITORING SETUP:                               │
    │                                                            │
    │   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │   │                                                    │   │
    │   │    ┌────────┐    ┌────────┐    ┌────────┐         │   │
    │   │    │VOLTMTR │    │AMMETER │    │WATTMTR │         │   │
    │   │    │ 24.5V  │    │  5.2A  │    │ 127W   │         │   │
    │   │    └────────┘    └────────┘    └────────┘         │   │
    │   │                                                    │   │
    │   │    ┌────────┐    ┌────────┐                       │   │
    │   │    │  RPM   │    │  Ah    │                       │   │
    │   │    │  285   │    │ 47.3   │                       │   │
    │   │    └────────┘    └────────┘                       │   │
    │   │                                                    │   │
    │   │    [LED: Charging]  [LED: Full]  [LED: Fault]     │   │
    │   │                                                    │   │
    │   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

SIMPLE VOLTAGE INDICATOR (No meter):

    Using LED ladder for approximate voltage indication:
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   VOLTAGE LADDER (12V nominal system):                     │
    │                                                            │
    │   INPUT+──┬──[R1]──[LED1]──┐   LED1 on = >10V (Low)       │
    │           │                │                               │
    │           ├──[R2]──[LED2]──┤   LED2 on = >11V (OK)        │
    │           │                │                               │
    │           ├──[R3]──[LED3]──┤   LED3 on = >12V (Good)      │
    │           │                │                               │
    │           └──[R4]──[LED4]──┤   LED4 on = >13V (Charging)  │
    │                            │                               │
    │   INPUT-───────────────────┘                               │
    │                                                            │
    │   Use Zener diodes in series with resistors to set        │
    │   threshold voltages for each LED.                        │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

SCAVENGED METERS:

    Sources for panel meters:
    - Old battery chargers (ammeter, voltmeter)
    - Multimeters (can mount permanently)
    - Car dashboard gauges
    - Solar charge controllers (have displays)
    - UPS units

17.1 EMERGENCY POWER KIT

SCENARIO: Power outage, natural disaster, camping, remote location

KIT CONTENTS:

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   EMERGENCY POWER KIT - TIER 0/1                           │
    │                                                            │
    │   GENERATION:                                              │
    │   □ TIER 0-1 hand-crank generator                         │

    │                                                            │
    │   STORAGE:                                                 │

    │   □ Spare rechargeable batteries (AA, AAA)                │
    │                                                            │
    │   OUTPUT:                                                  │
    │   □ USB cables (various types)                            │

    │                                                            │
    │   STORAGE CONTAINER:                                       │
    │   □ Waterproof case or bag                                │
    │   □ Padded for magnet protection                          │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

USAGE PROTOCOL:

    1. ASSESS POWER NEED
       - Phone charging: 5-15 minutes cranking = 5-10% charge
       - LED light: Continuous cranking or charge power bank first
       - Radio: Low power, direct operation possible
    
    2. PRIORITIZE LOADS
       - Communication first (phone, radio)
       - Lighting second
       - Other devices third
    
    3. CONSERVE ENERGY
       - Charge power bank, then charge devices from power bank
       - Use LED lighting (most efficient)
       - Turn off devices when not in use

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

SCENARIO: Schools, workshops, training

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   EDUCATIONAL DEMONSTRATION GENERATOR                      │
    │                                                            │
    │   FEATURES:                                                │
    │                                                            │
    │   □ Transparent cover (shows internal components)          │

    │   5. 3-phase vs single phase                              │
    │   6. Rectification (AC to DC conversion)                  │
    │   7. Filtering (smooth vs pulsating DC)                   │
    │   8. Coil turn effect (more turns → more voltage)         │
    │   9. Air gap effect (closer → more power)                 │
    │   10. Magnet strength effect                              │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

BUILD MODIFICATIONS FOR DEMONSTRATION:

    1. Use clear acrylic for frame plates
    2. Add colored wires (red, yellow, blue for phases)
    3. Install panel meters (voltmeter, ammeter)
    4. Add multiple output jacks (for different demonstrations)
    5. Include LED, small motor, buzzer as visual loads
    6. Make coils removable (quick-connect terminals)
    7. Add graduated scale for air gap adjustment

17.6 SURVIVAL SCENARIO APPLICATIONS
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

SCENARIO A: Extended Power Outage (Days to Weeks)

    PRIORITY ACTIONS:
    
    1. IMMEDIATE (First 24 hours)
       - Assess available materials for generator construction
       - Gather magnets from available electronics
       - Build TIER 0 generator for phone/radio charging
    
    2. SHORT-TERM (Days 2-7)
       - Upgrade to TIER 1 with more magnets/coils
       - Establish battery charging system
       - Set up LED lighting
    
    3. MEDIUM-TERM (Week 2+)
       - Build pedal-powered or wind-powered drive
       - Expand to TIER 2 for more capacity
       - Establish sustainable generation routine

SCENARIO B: Remote Location (No Grid Access)

    SYSTEM PROGRESSION:
    
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │                                                            │
    │   PHASE 1: BASIC POWER (Week 1-2)                         │
    │   - Build TIER 1 hand-crank generator                     │
    │   - Phone charging, LED lighting                          │
    │   - 1-2 hours cranking per day                            │
    │                                                            │
    │   PHASE 2: IMPROVED GENERATION (Week 3-4)                 │
    │   - Add pedal drive (bicycle frame)                       │
    │   - Add battery storage (car battery)                     │
    │   - Reduce manual effort, extend runtime                  │
    │                                                            │
    │   PHASE 3: RENEWABLE PRIMARY (Month 2+)                   │
    │   - Build wind turbine or water wheel                     │
    │   - Generator runs automatically                          │
    │   - Pedal becomes backup                                  │
    │   - Expand loads (fridge, laptop, router)                 │
    │                                                            │
    │   PHASE 4: FULL SYSTEM (Month 3+)                         │
    │   - Multiple generation sources                           │
    │   - Larger battery bank                                   │
    │   - Most household loads covered                          │
    │   - Surplus for community sharing                         │
    │                                                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

SCENARIO C: Disaster Relief/Humanitarian

    RAPID DEPLOYMENT GENERATOR:
    
    Requirements:
    - Must be buildable from common disaster debris
    - Must use minimal specialized tools
    - Must provide immediate benefit (phone charging)
    - Must be scalable with available materials
    
    Materials commonly available after disasters:
    - Car batteries (from damaged vehicles)
    - Motors/magnets (from appliances)
    - Wire (from buildings, vehicles)
    - Wood (from structures)
    - Bearings (from vehicles, appliances)
    
    Priority outputs:
    1. Communication (phone charging)
    2. Medical (device charging, lights for treatment)
    3. Lighting (security, operations)
    4. Water (pumping if needed)

                    ADVANCED TOPICS
